  - new `tests/integration/test_candidates.py` (8), with a test theme config without Lumentum and Soitec: the gate test (one discovery of the SearXNG fixture → `propose_candidates` → Lumentum (by ticker on Nasdaq → CIK, `sec`, linked to its two leads) and Soitec (name only → GLEIF LEI, FR, no source path) become `lead` Candidates; Coherent resolves to the universe, "Acme Photonics" is unresolved; nothing committed or enqueued); the extractor's request (quoted leads, strict schema, own run with its tokens); a lead examined once; commit → company in the database universe, `investigating`, audited, SEC ingest enqueued and run from the EDGAR fixtures, retained with `theme:photonics`; commit without a CIK → `ingest_note`, no job; reject keeps the reason, no second decision, `DELETE` refused by the trigger; refusals (`slug_taken`, `cik_not_offered`, 422s, 404); `atlas_candidates{state}`
  - changed: `test_migrations.py` head `0026`, `test_queue_pause.py` `PAUSABLE_KINDS` + `propose_candidates`
  - written against the design, then run: first run 1 failure (the test expected "Soitec SA" as the name; GLEIF's legal name is "SOITEC"); then `tests/integration/test_candidates.py` **8 passed**
  - local: ruff format/lint clean, `pyright` 0 errors, frontend gates and API client check passed in a local `ci.sh --no-image`, whose pytest stage I stopped (my PIDs only) when CI moved to the self-hosted GitHub runners; the full suite result is the GitHub run below
- **Fixture-only vs live:** everything. No LLM, SearXNG, SEC, GLEIF or OpenFIGI request was made; the extractor's answers are scripted in the test, SearXNG and GLEIF answers hand-written.
- **Deviations:**
  - Commit and reject apply only to a `lead`; the other §8.3 transitions are later tickets'.
  - A committed company without a CIK gets no source path and no ingest (`ingest_note`): exchange feeds need a config entry and a register decision.
  - A commit applies no CIK↔LEI link and no listings (identity review, ticket 02); an LEI-only company keeps its LEI.
  - An investigation's Scout (ticket 14) calls the discovery directly, so its leads aren't proposed automatically.
- **Next:** research-workflow transitions of Candidates; running identity resolution (`store_resolution`) for committed companies; a Candidates page in the frontend.
## 2026-09-29: Phase 3-6a ticket 16, Hypotheses

- **Built:**
  - `backend/atlas/hypotheses/`: `model.py` (the read side: status and allowed transitions, the drafting's state, immutable versions with content, SHA-256 and provenance, transitions; the §5.6 `TRANSITIONS`), `service.py` (`Hypotheses`: `create` (save a stopped investigation with a research card, enqueue the draft in the same transaction), `transition`, `correct` (a new version from the latest), `publish` behind a pluggable gate (`PublishCheck`s, default `requires_falsifier`, `requires_unresolved_question`; `PublishHook`s for ticket 20)), `findings.py` (a finding cites accepted Claims only, else it is unsupported), `drafting.py` (the `draft_hypothesis` job: the Editor's draft in its own `hypothesis_draft` run), `diff.py` (claims new / contradicted / unchanged / removed, fields changed), `export.py` (the JSON dossier with numbered citations and run metadata; Markdown rendering), `handlers.py` (pausable `draft_hypothesis`)
  - `roles/editor.py` `HYPOTHESIS_EDITOR` (the Editor role, recorded as `editor`) + `roles/prompts/editor-hypothesis.v1.md`
  - migration `0027` (down_revision `0023`): `hypothesis`, `hypothesis_version` (trigger `ENABLE ALWAYS`: only the publication stamp on an unpublished version; no delete/truncate), `hypothesis_transition` (insert-only)
  - `api/hypotheses.py`: `POST /api/v1/hypotheses` (202), `GET /hypotheses[/{id}]`, `POST /hypotheses/{id}/transitions`, `POST /hypotheses/{id}/versions` (201), `POST /hypotheses/{id}/publish-version`, `GET /hypotheses/{id}/diff`, `GET /hypotheses/{id}/export?format=json|markdown`; API client regenerated
  - ticket 12 follow-up: an investigation's final stop enqueues `review_relationships` for its accepted Claims' Assertions (`relationship_review_queued` event; `investigations/service.py`)
  - small additive edits: `investigations/tasks.py` (`accepted_claims`, `card_finding` made public for reuse), `jobs/handlers.py`, `api/app.py`
  - docs: `docs/decisions.md` entry (and ticket 12's "not automatic" note), `docs/data-model.md` §3.1g, `AGENTS.md`
- **Tests:**
  - new `tests/integration/test_hypotheses.py` (8): **the gate test**: an end-to-end fixture investigation (Scout → Investigator on Coherent's 10-K → Editor → chained Reviewer) saved and drafted into a reviewable Hypothesis: thesis, mechanism, ≥ 1 falsifier and unresolved question, the finding's span equal to the archived parse, findings citing a lead or nothing kept only as unsupported, the Editor sent the card, Claims and quotes as low-trust data, provenance (both runs, both Editor calls), audit, idempotent retry; the JSON and Markdown export (citations with quote, span, URL, tier, availability; runs and role calls with prompt SHA-256); the lifecycle (refused skips, `reviewed` only by publishing, publish only of the latest, transitions history) and **immutability in the database** (UPDATE/DELETE of a published version and DELETE of transitions refused); the publish gate (422 with both codes; a correction adds them as version 2, which publishes); corrections and the diff (unchanged / contradicted by a disputed Assertion / new / removed; `fields_changed`; unsupported correction 422; stale base 409; `hypothesis.corrected`); saving refused for a running, card-less, unknown or already-saved investigation; a draft quarantined on all 3 attempts marked `failed` with no version and its run finished; 503 without LiteLLM
  - `tests/integration/test_investigations.py`: +1 (a final stop queues the review of its Assertions, which runs in its own run and forms the machine-reviewed Coherent → NVIDIA edge; a `budget_exhausted` stop queues none); the 5 runs reaching a final stop with an accepted Claim now script the Reviewer's answer
  - `test_queue_pause.py`: `draft_hypothesis` pausable; `test_migrations.py`: head `0027`
  - the tests were written alongside the code, not run red first; the first run's one failure was the test's Editor helper citing leads
  - `tests/integration/test_hypotheses.py` 8 passed; `test_investigations.py`, `test_migrations.py`, `test_queue_pause.py` 27 passed; ruff, strict pyright and the API client check clean. A local `scripts/ci.sh --no-image` was stopped partway (~42%, no failures up to then) at the lead's request; the full suite runs on the self-hosted runners after the lead pushes the branch.
- **Fixture-only vs live:** all of it. Every role answer (Scout, Investigator, both Editors, Reviewer) is scripted; SearXNG and Hindsight are fakes. The `editor-hypothesis` prompt hasn't been tried on MiniMax.
- **Deviations:**
  - The diff has a fourth class, `removed` (dropped with no contradiction), beside the spec's new / contradicted / unchanged. Contradiction is read from the Assertions' review state when the diff is read (or from new counterevidence IDs, once the Skeptic fills them).
  - Every edit is a new version (unpublished ones never change either), rather than mutable drafts.
  - Publishing needs `evidence_ready` and moves the Hypothesis to `reviewed`; a correction published while `reviewed`/`paper_tracking` keeps the status. The gate today is only ≥ 1 falsifier and unresolved question; Relationship approval and the snapshot are ticket 20. There is no separate `enroll-paper-tracking` endpoint yet: `transitions {to: paper_tracking}`.
  - The chained relationship review runs in its own `relationship_review` run, not the investigation's (finished by the same stop), so its tokens aren't counted against the investigation's budget.
  - The draft job runs under `ATLAS_RUN_TOKEN_BUDGET` in its own run, not the investigation's budget.
- **Next:** ticket 20 adds the Relationship-approval check and the snapshot hook to the gate; ticket 21 flags contradictions on published versions; ticket 24 builds the dossier page on the export; ticket 15's counterevidence feeds the diff's `contradicted`.

## 2026-09-29: Phase 3-6a ticket 27, quota-window pacing and staged universe rollout

- **Built:**
  - `atlas.jobs.budget`: job kinds by provider (`codex`: retain, poll_operation, reprocess, refresh_mental_model, reflect; `minimax`: discover, extract_claims, review_relationships, investigation_task), rolling-window budgets (Codex in Hindsight operations submitted, MiniMax in recorded `llm_call` tokens), the interactive reserve for backfill, and when each class resumes. Every claim records unseen usage in `provider_usage` at the pacing clock, then skips kinds whose provider window is spent for the job's class. `poll_operation` is never held.
  - `atlas.jobs.pacing`: `ATLAS_BACKFILL_WINDOW` is now empty by default (any time) and accepts comma-separated ranges; `Pacing` carries the budgets.
  - Settings: `ATLAS_BUDGET_WINDOW_HOURS` (5), `ATLAS_CODEX_BUDGET_OPERATIONS` (40), `ATLAS_MINIMAX_BUDGET_TOKENS` (400000), `ATLAS_BUDGET_INTERACTIVE_RESERVE` (0.3).
  - Migration `0028` (down_revision `0023`): `provider_usage` (existing operations and LLM calls recorded at their own times) and `ingest_plan`.
  - `atlas.ledger.plans` + `GET /api/v1/ingest-plans[?company=]`: a company's first backfill SEC ingest records the discovered documents, their count and the estimated retain operations before any fetch (audited `ingest_plan.recorded`).
  - `atlas ingest --max-retains N` (SEC filers only): retains at most N not-yet-retained versions of the company, newest first; artifacts show `retains_deferred`; a rerun takes the next N (`atlas.retention.service.unretained_versions`).
  - `GET /api/v1/queue`: `budgets` per provider and `budget_held` per pending kind. Metrics: `atlas_budget_used`, `atlas_budget_limit`, `atlas_budget_window_seconds`, `atlas_budget_usage_total`, `atlas_queue_jobs_held_by_budget`.
  - Docs: `docs/runbooks.md` "Universe rollout and quota budgets" (reading the view, tuning, the order for the ten remaining companies), `docs/decisions.md`, data model, architecture, threat model, `.env.example`, AGENTS.md; API client regenerated.
- **Files:** `backend/atlas/jobs/{budget,pacing,queue,__init__}.py`, `backend/atlas/ledger/{plans,ingest}.py`, `backend/atlas/retention/service.py`, `backend/atlas/api/{queue,ingest_plans,app}.py`, `backend/atlas/{metrics,settings,cli}.py`, `backend/atlas/db/migrations/versions/0028_quota_windows.py`, `tests/integration/test_quota_pacing.py` (new), `tests/integration/{test_queue_pause,test_migrations}.py`, `tests/unit/test_settings.py`, `frontend/lib/api/`.
- **Tests (worker-pass seam, injectable clock):** `test_quota_pacing.py` 6 passed locally: a backfill over the Codex budget spreads over two windows while an interactive ingest runs in the reserve (queue view, metrics, resume time); a 429 still pauses under the budgets; MiniMax role calls held by their recorded tokens (backfill held, interactive in the reserve, resumes after 5 h); the first backfill ingest records its plan before any fetch or retain and honours the cap, a rerun retains the deferred version; only a first backfill ingest records a plan; the cap is refused for a non-SEC company. `test_queue_pause.py` window tests 3 passed (a multi-range case added); `test_settings.py` 26 passed. ruff and strict pyright clean. The full suite runs on the owner's GitHub runners (below).
- **Fixture-tested only:** everything; Hindsight, LiteLLM and SearXNG are the recorded/scripted fakes. The default budgets are not calibrated against the real subscriptions.
- **Deviations:** a job may overshoot its provider's limit by what it spends itself (its cost is known only afterwards), once per concurrent worker. Mental-model refreshes and Hindsight consolidation spend Codex without an operation Atlas can count, so they aren't in the Codex usage (they are held when the window is spent). Exchange ingests record no plan and take no cap yet.
- **Next:** the owner runs the rollout per the runbook; calibrate the budgets after a few windows.

## 2026-09-29: ticket 13, edge table page (C)

- **Built:**
  - `frontend/app/relationships/`: the edge table (subject, predicate, object, layer, review state with its reasons and the owner's decision, Evidence and family counts). Every column header is a sort button (`aria-sort`); a second press reverses the order. The layer and review-state filters are selects. Filters and sort live in the query string (`lib/relationships.ts`), so the table survives a reload and can be linked.
  - `frontend/app/relationship/`: one edge, its owner review (approve/reject with an optional note, audited by the API), and its Evidence. Each supporting Assertion shows its quote, source (tier, Evidence Family) and machine review (checks, cue, hedge, Reviewer), and links to its source span.
  - `frontend/app/exceptions/`: the exceptions queue with the reasons and inline approve/reject; a decided edge leaves the queue.
  - Version page: `?assertion=<id>` highlights that Assertion's span (`<mark>`, scrolled into view) after checking the quote is at its offsets, and says so. Otherwise it shows an alert. `lib/offsets.ts` gained `splitAtSpan`, and the selection code already handles text split across elements.
  - `lib/api/client.ts`: the relationship and assertion calls. Site nav: Companies, Relationships, Exceptions queue.
  - `scripts/e2e.py`: seeds the company universe (`atlas companies seed`). It then builds three Relationships through the real services: Assertions on three hedged sentences of the Lumentum FY2026 10-K (`Assertions.create`), and one `review_relationships` job on the queue run by a `Worker` pass with `RelationshipReviewer`. Hedged quotes fail the deterministic checks, so the Reviewer is never asked; its role caller is a `MockTransport` that aborts the seed if called. The owner then approves the `manufactures` edge (`Relationships.review`).
- **API:** unchanged. Ticket 12's list, sort, filter, exceptions and review routes cover the page, so the generated client needed no regeneration (`gen_api_client.sh --check` passes).
- **Tests:**
  - `frontend/e2e/relationships.spec.ts` (2 tests): sort by object in both directions and by layer (upstream order); filter by review state and layer, surviving a reload; open an edge; open its span, check the highlighted `<mark>` is the quote at its offsets and in the viewport. Then in the exceptions queue: reject one edge with a note, approve the other, the queue empties and stays empty on reload, and the rejected edge shows in the filtered table; re-decide it on the edge page. `uv run python scripts/e2e.py` (with `PLAYWRIGHT_HOST_PLATFORM_OVERRIDE=ubuntu22.04-x64`): **6 passed** (the 4 existing ones too).
  - `frontend/unit/relationships.test.ts` (3) and `splitAtSpan` in `offsets.test.ts` (1): `npm run test` **15 passed**. lint, typecheck and build are clean.
  - Local `scripts/ci.sh --no-image`: ruff, pyright, the frontend gates and the API client check passed, and pytest **751 passed, 10 deselected**. The run was stopped during its e2e step when CI moved to the self-hosted runners; the GitHub Actions run on the pushed branch is the gate.
- **Fixture-only vs live:** everything is from the recorded EDGAR fixtures. No LLM was called; the seed's edges are all hedged, so no Reviewer call is needed. No `machine_reviewed` edge is seeded, since that needs a Reviewer answer.
- **Deviations:** none from the ticket. The edge table shows the API's largest page (500 edges) with its total; there's no paging UI yet.
- **Next:** the Theme explorer and Company dossier pages can link into `/relationships/` (the query string is the filter), and a `company_id` filter select is a small addition when they need one.

  - `tests/integration/test_universe.py`: Soitec's source path; the two "no adapter" tests removed (every source path has an adapter now); the ignored-ADR-CIK test also drops Soitec's `exchange` block. `tests/integration/test_identity.py`: its test config uses `exchange:amf`
  - The adapter and integration tests were written against the fixtures before running and passed on their first run (no red run recorded as a whole module); the ignored-ADR-CIK test failed once (Soitec's new `exchange` block with `source_path: sec`) and passed after the fix.
  - Local: `uv run pytest tests/unit/test_amf_adapter.py tests/unit/test_fetch_gate.py tests/unit/test_fca_nsm_adapter.py tests/unit/test_hkexnews_adapter.py tests/integration/test_amf.py tests/integration/test_migrations.py tests/integration/test_universe.py tests/integration/test_identity.py tests/integration/test_fca_nsm.py tests/integration/test_hkexnews.py`: **117 passed**; `ruff format --check`, `ruff check` (backend, tests, the new script) and `pyright`: clean. Full CI: on the runners (the lead).
- **Fixture-only vs live:** all tests are fixture-only. To confirm the dataset and field names I made six live requests on 2026-09-29 with the generic `AtlasResearch` User-Agent: `www.info-financiere.gouv.fr/robots.txt`; the catalog (`/catalog/datasets?limit=20`); the `flux-amf-new-prod` dataset schema; one records search for Soitec's LEI (20 rows, `total_count` 277; headers `X-RateLimit-Limit: 5000` and a v2.0 deprecation notice); `fr.ftp.opendatasoft.com/robots.txt`; one HEAD of a Soitec English trading-update PDF (`application/pdf`, `Last-Modified`, `ETag`). Plus `www.euronext.com/robots.txt`, and the data.gouv.fr dataservice page and Euronext's terms page read with a fetch tool; Soitec's terms are quoted from web-search text (its page wasn't fetched). No document was downloaded and no live ingest was run.
- **Deviations:**
  - robots.txt disallows `/api/` for `*` on the API host and everything on the file host. Following the ticket ("Atlas is an API client, not a crawler"), the gate gained `api_client` so those exact prefixes are allowed with the robots verdict recorded; extending that to the file host (where every document lives) is my reading of the lead's decision and should be confirmed.
  - `rate_per_s` 0.5 (one request every 2 s, like the NSM), not lower: the job lease is 300 s and is not renewed, so a two-year first backfill (~150 files) is already ~5 min; the API quota is protected by the 10-call cap per discovery rather than by the rate. The runbook says to use `--limit` or a longer `ATLAS_JOB_LEASE_SECONDS` for the first backfill.
  - The API is used at v2.0 as data.gouv.fr documents it, though it announces v2.1 as current. The API's own rate-limit header says 5,000/day, not the documented 10,000.
  - AMF-published threshold-crossing decisions filed under Soitec's LEI are kept (French, archived, not retained) rather than filtered by diffuser code, whose meaning isn't documented.
  - `source_path` `exchange:euronext` is removed rather than kept as a blocked path (as 0025 did for `lse-rns`); the block lives in the register. With that, the "no adapter" refusal in `exchange_refusal` has no source path left; the code stays, its two tests are removed.
  - `issuer_code` in the announcement metadata is the LEI (like the NSM); `announcement_id` is `uin_idt_uin`.
- **Next:** a first live AMF ingest of Soitec (`ATLAS_EXCHANGE_LIVE=true`) with the owner's go-ahead, with `--limit` or a longer lease; the lead confirms the `api_client` exemption for the file host; consider moving to Explore v2.1 when v2.0 is retired.
## 2026-09-30: Phase 3-6a ticket 23, Theme explorer (A) and Company dossier (B)

- **Built:**
  - `backend/atlas/themes.py`: the Theme explorer's reads. A theme is the universe config's, extended with committed Candidates (`extend_universe`). Its companies are grouped by layer, upstream to downstream (`LAYERS`), empty layers included (a coverage gap). Each company shows `seeded`, its Source Document, Relationship and pending-identity-review counts, and `gaps` (`not_seeded`, `no_sources`). The map also holds the Relationships between the theme's companies (subject in the theme; object a theme company or a product, sorted by layer) and the theme's Candidates. The summary gives company count, companies without sources, empty layers, the Relationship count and open (`lead`) Candidates.
  - `backend/atlas/dossier.py`: the Company dossier in one read. It gathers identity and listings (`get_company`), pending identity mappings, themes, Source Documents (with the total), Relationships out (as subject) and in (as object), `financial_figures` as of `as_of`, and blocked fetch-gate decisions (with the total). Every part is the same read its own route serves.
  - API (`api/themes.py`, `api/dossier.py`, registered in `api/app.py`):
    - `GET /api/v1/themes` and `GET /api/v1/themes/{id}/map` (404 `not_found`). The map adds `bottlenecks`, which is the Bottlenecks mental model read through `MentalModelReader`, so its citations resolve as a reflect answer's do. Its `status` is `refreshed`, `not_refreshed`, `not_in_bank`, `not_configured` or `unavailable` (with the message). A Hindsight failure never fails the map.
    - `GET /api/v1/companies/{id}/dossier?as_of=` (404, 422).
    - The API client is regenerated.
  - Frontend:
    - `app/themes/`: the themes with their coverage.
    - `app/theme/?id=`: one table per layer with an explicit "Coverage gap" for an empty layer, the edges (`components/edge-list.tsx`, rows named by the edge and opening its page), the Candidates, and the open gaps. The gaps are labelled "Generated by Hindsight: Memory, not Evidence" with citation counts, "Unsupported" when no citation resolves, and links to the resolved sections.
    - `app/company/?id=` is now the dossier: identity, listings, pending identity reviews, Relationships out and in, financials, Source Documents and fetch-gate blocks. Each financial figure shows its period, unit, FX basis ("as filed"), `available_at`, source form, accession and concept (or "derived from"), and flags.
    - `lib/format.ts` groups digits without floats. The site nav gains Themes, and `lib/routes.ts` gains `themes` and `theme`.
  - `scripts/e2e.py` is unchanged: its seed (Lumentum ingest with companyfacts, three hedged Relationships) already covers the pages.
- **Tests:**
  - New `tests/integration/test_theme_explorer.py` (13), at the HTTP API seam:
    - Themes coverage before and after seeding and ingest.
    - Layers and members equal the theme config, and gaps are right before and after seeding.
    - Map edges exclude an edge to a company outside the theme (NVIDIA); counts include it.
    - Only the theme's Candidates are listed.
    - Unknown theme: 404.
    - Bottlenecks: `not_configured`, `not_refreshed` after `apply-template`, and `refreshed` after the fake's `apply_refresh`, with content and `evidence_missing`. A Hindsight outage gives `unavailable` and the map still loads.
    - Dossier identity, listings, themes, sources (equal to `/sources`), and edges out and in.
    - Dossier financials as of the cutoff: FY2026 revenue 3,014.0M, available at 2026-08-17T20:03:17Z, is absent just before that.
    - Pending identity review; fetch-gate block; 404/422.
    - The state comes from the CLI seed, an ingest worker pass, the Assertions service with a `review_relationships` job (hedged, so the Reviewer is never asked), and `record_decision`. **A Candidate row and a pending identity-mapping row are inserted by SQL**, since their flows are tickets 09 and 02. Result: **13 passed**.
    - Not strictly red-first: the reads were written just before the tests.
  - New `frontend/e2e/theme-explorer.spec.ts` (1): Themes nav, the coverage row (12 / 11 / none / 3 / 0), the theme, layer headings in order, chip/laser members and gaps, three edges, "Hindsight is not configured", then Lumentum's dossier (CIK, theme link, LITE, three edges out, the FY2026 revenue row with its 10-K accession, Source Documents), then an edge, its "Open source span" and the highlighted `<mark>` in the viewport. It uses only auto-retrying `expect(locator)` assertions. `uv run python scripts/e2e.py` (with `PLAYWRIGHT_HOST_PLATFORM_OVERRIDE=ubuntu22.04-x64`): **7 passed** (the 6 existing ones too, with the company page now the dossier).
  - New `frontend/unit/format.test.ts` (2). `npm run test`: **17 passed**. lint, typecheck and build are clean.
  - `uv run ruff format --check` / `ruff check` on backend, tests and scripts, and `uv run pyright`: clean. `scripts/gen_api_client.sh --check`: current. `uv run pytest tests/unit`: 411 passed. The full suite is left to the runners.
- **Fixture-only vs live:** everything is fixture-tested (the recorded EDGAR fixtures, the Hindsight fake's derived template import and refresh). No LLM, Hindsight or SEC call was made.
- **Deviations:**
  - `GET /themes/{id}/map` takes no `as_of` (product spec §11 lists one), and there are no time controls or search on the explorer yet. The dossier's `as_of` applies to its financials only.
  - The map's edges are gathered per member company, up to 500 each, and the dossier's lists up to 500 each (their totals are given for sources and blocks). That fits the pilot's scale; there is no paging UI.
  - The dossier shows no timeline, Hindsight memory results or Hypotheses yet (§10.1 B lists them); those belong to later pages or tickets.
  - Candidates on the map are read-only; commit and reject stay API-only.
- **Next:** an `as_of` on the map (edges and sources as known at the cutoff); a company filter link from the dossier to the edge table (`/relationships/?company_id=`); the dossier's Hypotheses once ticket 24's page exists.
## 2026-09-30: Phase 3-6a ticket 30, retention triage

- **Built:**
  - `atlas.roles.triage` + `roles/prompts/triage.v1.md`: the Triage role (strict schema: per anchor `retain`/`skip`, a value category from the rubric's list, a one-line reason). The prompt is the versioned rubric (`triage.v1`). Section openings go in as quoted, low-trust `retrieved_data`; the document's metadata and its company's themes go in the request.
  - `atlas.retention.triage`: the `triage` job (a `minimax` kind in `atlas.jobs.budget`, pausable). For each undecided section it applies, in order: inherit from the predecessor version by section SHA-256; the boilerplate rules `triage-rules-v1` (Item headings only); then role calls, `ATLAS_TRIAGE_SECTIONS_PER_CALL` per call, in one `triage` run. If a section gets no answer, or its call is quarantined or out of token budget, it is retained (`default`). Once every section is decided, the job enqueues `retain:{id}:triaged`, or nothing if every section was skipped. `request_retain` does retain on demand.
  - `atlas.retention.decisions` + migration `0033` (down_revision `0028`): `triage_decision`, insert-only (the ledger triggers) and audited. The effective decision is the latest row. An advisory lock per version serializes the triage job and on-demand requests.
  - `atlas.retention.service`: `retain` with triage on hands an undecided version to `triage` (`awaiting_triage`). It records and submits only `retain` sections (`nothing_to_retain` when none), and any section retained on demand later. With triage off, and with no decisions, the behaviour is unchanged. `load_version`, `version_sections` and `is_retainable` are now module functions.
  - API: `GET /api/v1/triage` (filters `source_version_id`, `company_id`, `decision`, `category`, `method`, `effective`) and `POST /api/v1/source-versions/{id}/sections/{anchor}/retain` (202; `{reason, investigation_id?}`; 404 or 409 `already_retained`/`linked`/`not_retainable`). The API client was regenerated.
  - Metrics: `atlas_triage_sections{decision,category}`, `atlas_triage_decisions{method}`, `atlas_triage_hindsight_operations_saved_estimate{unit=documents|batches}`.
  - Settings: `ATLAS_RETENTION_TRIAGE=auto|on|off` (auto means on with LiteLLM), `ATLAS_TRIAGE_EXCERPT_CHARS` (1500), `ATLAS_TRIAGE_SECTIONS_PER_CALL` (15). Docs: `docs/decisions.md` "Retention triage", AGENTS.md, `.env.example`.
- **Files:** `backend/atlas/retention/{triage,decisions}.py` (new), `backend/atlas/retention/{service,handlers,__init__}.py`, `backend/atlas/roles/triage.py`, `backend/atlas/roles/prompts/triage.v1.md` (new), `backend/atlas/api/triage.py` (new), `backend/atlas/api/app.py`, `backend/atlas/{metrics,settings}.py`, `backend/atlas/jobs/budget.py`, `backend/atlas/db/migrations/versions/0033_triage_decisions.py` (new), `tests/integration/test_retention_triage.py` (new), `tests/integration/{test_migrations,test_queue_pause,test_quota_pacing}.py`, `tests/unit/test_settings.py`, `tests/harness.py` and `tests/live/test_phase2_gate_live.py` (both pin `retention_triage: off`), `frontend/lib/api/`.
- **Tests (worker-pass seam, scripted LiteLLM chat fake, recorded Hindsight fake):** `test_retention_triage.py`, **8 passed** locally:
  - boilerplate (10-K Items 1B/4/6, 8-K 9.01) is skipped by rule and never sent to the role, and the role sees each section's 1,500-character opening and the document's metadata;
  - only `retain` sections reach Hindsight, a version with every section skipped submits nothing, and a skipped section still takes an Assertion;
  - Coherent's Item 1, which names the NVIDIA supply agreement, is retained;
  - in a revised 8-K, the unchanged cover and Item 9.01 inherit and only the changed Item 2.02 is asked about;
  - retain on demand (recorded with the actor and reason, retained by the next worker pass, then 409/404/422), and with an investigation (unknown one 404);
  - the list filters, the audit rows, insert-only enforcement and the metrics;
  - `off` with LiteLLM configured retains every section with no call.
  
  `test_settings.py` 32 passed. ruff format/check and strict pyright are clean; the frontend typecheck is clean. `test_retention`, `test_migrations`, `test_queue_pause` and `test_quota_pacing`: **34 passed** locally. The full suite runs on the owner's runners.
- **Fixture-tested only:** everything. The Triage role's answers are hand-written in the test (a stand-in model), not MiniMax output. The rubric's real selectivity and token cost on MiniMax are unmeasured.
- **Deviations:**
  - The `triage` job is enqueued by the `retain` job (a `codex` kind), not by the ingest, so enqueue sites and `unretained_versions` are unchanged. Triage therefore waits when the Codex window is spent.
  - "Previous version" is the same Source Document's previous triaged version, else the company's previous triaged document of the same form and document type.
  - Rules apply to Item headings only, not to chunk first lines.
  - Triage fails open (retain) on a missing answer, quarantine or an exhausted run budget.
  - On-demand decisions use category `on_demand`.
  - Investigations don't call `request_retain` automatically yet; it accepts `investigation_id` for when they do.
- **Next:** measure the rubric on real filings with the live smoke setup (owner's go-ahead), then tune `triage.v1` and the rule list. The owner could expose triage decisions in the source viewer.

Form S-3, 424B, ATM programs and convertibles, customer concentration; research note M6, M10), each with a small cue list, and two role contracts recorded as `skeptic`: `SKEPTIC_PLAN` (prompt `skeptic-plan.v1.md`: its own queries and the catalog documents to read) and `SKEPTIC` (prompt `skeptic.v1.md`: counterevidence from quoted passages).
  - `backend/atlas/investigations/skeptic.py`: the Skeptic task. Plan (question, checklist, supporting Claims as request fields, a catalog of archived Tier A Source Versions of the investigation's and theme's companies as of the as-of time; **no Memory sent or read**), its own SearXNG search in a discovery of the run (results are Tier C leads; a result whose canonical URL is a catalog document adds it, `selected_by: search`), checklist-cue passages (round-robin over documents and items), batched reading with per-batch progress (resumable after a pause or a budget stop). Checks per item, including `not_a_witness` (only a passage of a chosen Source Version sent in that call is a witness: Memory, leads, other roles' Claims never are), `unknown_claim`, `unknown_premise`, quote placement, the span check and `party_not_in_quote`. Accepted items are Assertions (predicate `counterevidence`, extractor `skeptic.v1`, `atlas-skeptic`); **independent** only when their Evidence Family is none of the supporting Claims'. Accepted, independent counterevidence naming an open company premise disproves it (`atlas-skeptic`, event with the counterevidence IDs, audited); the question premise stays the researcher's.

  - Read side: `GET /api/v1/investigations/{id}` has `counterevidence` (every item, accepted or rejected with its reason, its family and independence); the research card has `contradictions`; a finding's `counterevidence_ids` lists the independent counterevidence against its Claims and makes it `needs_review`; a contradicted finding stops the investigation `needs_review`.
  - Editor prompts v2 (`editor.v2.md`, `editor-hypothesis.v2.md`): both Editors are sent the counterevidence (quotes as low-trust data), never citable as claims. The Hypothesis draft carries the card's `contradictions`, its findings' `counterevidence_ids` (kept by corrections), and the Markdown dossier lists them.
  - Migration `0030` (down_revision `0028`): `skeptic_search`, `counterevidence` (insert-only by trigger). `Investigations.disprove_within` (the disprove in a caller's transaction). API client regenerated. AGENTS.md.

- **Tests:**
  - `test_investigations.py` +4: the Skeptic's own query and search (its discovery, Tier C leads, the 10-Q found by search is read), its plan with no retrieved data, passages of the chosen document only, an accepted `counterevidence` Assertion and a rejected paraphrase, both visible on the investigation, the Editor sent it, the card's contradiction and the contradicted finding, the `needs_review` stop; **a finding sharing an Evidence Family with the Investigator's Evidence is not independent** (a byte-for-byte copy of the 10-K imported at another URL: accepted, `independent: false`, not counted against the finding and unable to disprove the premise); **Memory text is never a witness** (a real recalled memory, the Investigator's Claim and the Bottlenecks mental model cited as witnesses are all rejected `not_a_witness`; the Skeptic made no recall and read no mental model); the Skeptic's independent counterevidence disproves a company premise (not the question), cancelling what depends on it. ## 2026-09-30: Merge of tickets 15 (Skeptic) and 19 (scenarios, Financial Analyst)

- **Plan:** both slots run: Scout → Investigator(s) → Skeptic ∥ Financial Analyst → Editor (with the Skeptic's counterevidence). The two sibling jobs are enqueued in one transaction, so the queue claims them in either order (same `created_at`, then the job's uuid5 id).
- **Resume fix:** a `budget_exhausted` stop by one sibling left the other `queued` with a job the worker had already finished without running it (the investigation was stopped), so resume never re-queued it. `resume` now also gives such a task a new generation and job (`investigations/service.py`).
- **Tests:** `tests/fakes/litellm.py` gained `script_role` (a role's replies, answered before the `script_chat` queue); the investigation, hypothesis and scenario tests script the Skeptic and the Analyst by role and compare their calls in plan order. The two budget-before-the-sibling tests became one that accepts either sibling spending the budget and checks both run after resume.
- **Migrations renumbered to follow the chain** (old → new): `0033_triage_decisions` → `0030`, `0030_counterevidence` → `0031`, `0031_scenarios` → `0032`; head `0032`. Earlier entries in this log keep the old numbers.

## 2026-09-30: ticket 30 follow-up, retention triage holds and retries instead of failing open

- **Built:** owner decision 2026-09-30. `atlas.retention.triage` no longer retains a section "by default" when triage fails:
  - an LLM outage or quota failure still raises `TransientFailure` from the role caller: the queue pauses and the job is requeued without using an attempt;
  - the triage run's own token budget spent (`TokenBudgetExhausted`) pauses nothing. The lead ruled that this budget is per-run, not the provider's. The handler raises the new `atlas.jobs.pacing.Requeue`; the worker calls the new `JobQueue.requeue`, which puts the job back without using an attempt and records a failure classed `requeued` (added to `FailureClassification`; API client regenerated). The next attempt starts a fresh run once the `minimax` window budget (ticket 27) has room;
  - a quarantined answer, or sections the answer leaves out, stay undecided. The remaining batches still run, then the attempt raises `TriageIncomplete` (a `RoleCallFailed`, an ordinary failure), retried up to `max_attempts` and then failed visibly. If the run's budget runs out after an undecided section, the attempt fails the same way, not requeued: otherwise a repeatedly quarantined batch would loop without using attempts;
  - decisions already made stay (insert-only); a retry asks only about the undecided sections. The artifacts' tally drops `default`. The `default` method stays in the schema for older rows (no migration). `ATLAS_RETENTION_TRIAGE=off` is unchanged;
  - `atlas triage retry --source-version <id> | --failed` (`atlas.retention.retry_failed_triage`) re-enqueues the triage of versions whose latest triage job failed, in its class and with its `max_attempts`, keyed `triage:{id}:retry:{n}` (audited as `job.enqueued`). It exits 2 for a version whose latest triage job didn't fail. The gauge `atlas_triage_failed_jobs` counts versions whose latest triage job failed.
- **Files:** `backend/atlas/retention/{triage,__init__}.py`, `backend/atlas/jobs/{pacing,queue,worker,__init__}.py`, `backend/atlas/{cli,metrics}.py`, `frontend/lib/api/{openapi.json,schema.ts}`, `tests/integration/test_retention_triage.py`, `docs/decisions.md` ("Retention triage": "Hold and retry" replaces "Fail open"), this log.
- **Tests (worker-pass and CLI seams, scripted LiteLLM chat fake, recorded Hindsight fake):** three new tests in `test_retention_triage.py`. The hold-and-retry assertions failed before the change; the requeue, retry and metric assertions were added with their code, not run red first.
  - Every answer is invalid: each of the 4 triage jobs fails after 3 attempts (24 chat completions: call and repair per attempt). Only `rule` decisions are recorded, nothing reaches Hindsight, and `atlas_triage_failed_jobs` is 4. `atlas triage retry --source-version <10-K>` then triages and retains only the 10-K (gauge 3), and a second retry of it exits 2. `--failed` retries the other 3 (gauge 0), and then enqueues nothing.
  - The 10-K's first answer leaves out Item 1: the job fails once ("no decision"), the retry asks only about `part-i-item-1`, which is then retained, and each 10-K section has exactly one decision.
  - `ATLAS_RUN_TOKEN_BUDGET` and `ATLAS_MINIMAX_BUDGET_TOKENS` are set to one call's tokens, on an injectable pacing clock. Each worker pass makes one call. The queue is never paused (`pauses_total` 0), yet with the window spent triage is `budget_held` and makes no call. After each 5 h window rolls, it makes one more call. The 10-K's job is requeued mid-way (`requeued` failures naming the token budget; `attempts` stays 1) and resumes part-decided, with nothing retained for it in between. No section of a version is asked twice, and there are no `default` decisions. In the end only Item 1 is retained.

  `test_retention_triage.py`: **11 passed**. `test_cli` (unit), `test_jobs`, `test_queue_pause`, `test_quota_pacing` and `test_retention`: **53 passed**. ruff format/check (backend, tests, scripts) and strict pyright are clean. The API client was regenerated with the main checkout's `openapi-typescript` 7.13.0, since the worktree has no `node_modules`. The full suite runs on the owner's runners.
- **Fixture-tested only:** everything; no LLM or Hindsight call was made.
- **Deviations:** a `Requeue`d job is claimable again straight away. It has no delay of its own; only the `minimax` window budget (and the backfill window) hold it. Each retry starts a fresh run, whose first call always goes out, so every requeue makes progress.
- **Next:** an alert rule on `atlas_triage_failed_jobs > 0` in `configs/prometheus/atlas-alerts.yaml` if the owner wants paging.
## 2026-09-30: Phase 3-6a ticket 25, the evaluation set

- **Built:**

Eleven are synthetic, about fictional companies (Aurora Photonics, Borealis Substrates, Cirrus Compute, Dunmore Epitaxy, Ember Lasers; `.example` URLs, CIKs `09999000NN`). One (RST) reuses the hand-written Nokia EDGAR sample (`public_regulatory`). The cases:
    - `EV-SUP-001` a correct directed supplier edge (`supplies`, substrate layer, machine-reviewed, citation resolves);
    - `EV-SUP-002` a `buys_from` stated by the buyer (and never the reverse);
    - `EV-DIR-001` the reversed-direction trap: the reverse is proposed, and the Reviewer's `reversed` keeps it `not_verified`;
    - `EV-COM-001` a trade-show co-mention and `EV-COM-002` a market ranking (rejected `no_directional_language`; no edge);
    - `EV-INF-001` a partner page, "works with", and a supplier removed in a second version (no edge; `party_not_in_quote`);
    - `EV-LAY-001` substrate vs epi (the substrate edge is verified; the epi one waits on `layer_not_confirmed`);
    - `EV-SYN-001` one release with ten mirrors is one Evidence Family, and an unrelated release is a second;
    - `EV-HED-001` "in discussions / expect to / non-binding" goes to the exceptions queue with `hedged_language`;
    - `EV-FUT-001` an 8-K published after the as-of time (for an event before it) never reaches the investigation (`no_new_independent_evidence`, forbidden source unused);
    - `EV-RST-001` Nokia's FY2023 revenue as of before and after the restating 20-F (22,258 then 21,138 EUR million, `restates`);
    - `EV-CON-001` the Skeptic finds a later 10-Q contradicting the supply claim: an independent contradiction on the card, stop `needs_review`.
  - **`atlas.evaluation`:**
    - `gold.py`: the Pydantic case and manifest models and the §9 validator (ID pattern, hashes of cases and sources, licence classes, gold quotes occurring exactly in the parse, every entity and source key defined, including the script's).
    - `stubs.py`: localhost stubs for Hindsight (version `evaluation-stub`, template import, empty recall) and SearXNG (no results), and the scripted LiteLLM. Its answers are written per role in the case's terms and computed against the request Atlas sent. The Investigator proposes only quotes a sent passage holds.
    - `runner.py`: one database per case, cloned from a migrated template per run and dropped afterwards; manual imports or the EDGAR fixture path; the real job handlers in worker passes; observation through the read side.
    - `scoring.py`: the checks, each feeding a methodology §7 metric.
    - `store.py`: runs and results.

  - **CLI:** `atlas evaluate [--case ID]... [--live]` prints one line per case to stderr and the run as JSON. Exit 0 when all pass, 1 on a failed case, 2 when refused.
  - **API:** `GET /api/v1/evaluations[/{id}]` (per-category tallies, every check with what was expected and observed, per-metric scores, the predicted output).

## 2026-09-30: Phase 3-6a ticket 17, the research workbench and the follow-up round

- **Built:**
  - `POST /api/v1/investigations/{id}/follow-up {question}` (`Investigations.follow_up`): one bounded follow-up round on one of the research card's open questions. Round 2 gets the same fixed plan (new `investigation_task` rows, same premises) in the same run: the run is reopened, so the round spends what is left of the token, lead and document budgets. It is refused with 409 while the investigation runs, after a budget or premise stop, when the rounds (`round_budget_spent`) or tokens are spent, and once the investigation is saved as a Hypothesis (`hypothesis_saved`). A question that isn't on the card is 422 `unknown_open_question`. The follow-up is recorded in the insert-only `investigation_follow_up` table (migration `0036`) with the card as it stood before the round, plus a `follow_up_started` event and an `investigation.follow_up_started` audit event.
  - `tasks.py`: the round's Scout searches for the open question, and its Investigators use the open question for recall and read only Source Versions the investigation hasn't read (`investigation_document`). The Editor's existing rule on new Evidence Families decides whether the card is redrafted. `service.py`: a final stop queues relationship review for its own round's Assertions only (round 2's jobs are keyed `investigation:<id>:round-2:<n>`; round 1's keys are unchanged).
  - Read side: `GET /api/v1/investigations` (newest first), and on `GET /investigations/{id}`: `evidence` (the Evidence tray: accepted Claims with names, Assertion, span, source, family, round, `excluded`), `follow_ups`, and `request.hypothesis_id` filled from the saved Hypothesis.
  - Frontend: `app/workbench/` lists investigations and has a start form (theme, question, seed companies from the theme map). `app/investigation/?id=` shows status and budget use; resume (budget stops), the follow-up round (an open-question radio group) and save as Hypothesis; the plan by round with each task's status, detail and output (generic over artifacts, so the Financial Analyst slot needs no change when ticket 19 lands); premises with disprove; the Evidence tray with "Open source span"; accepted counterevidence; the research card and open questions; leads and documents read; and the event stream. The page polls every 2 s while the investigation runs (`usePolled` in `lib/use-api.ts`), and there is a nav link. The pure parts are in `lib/workbench.ts`. `lib/api/client.ts` adds the investigation and Hypothesis calls, and `Posted` accepts 202. API client regenerated.
  - `scripts/e2e.py`: a second database and API for the workbench. The recorded Hindsight, LiteLLM and SearXNG fakes (`tests/fakes`) are served on localhost; the script migrates, applies the template, seeds the universe plus NVIDIA, and ingests and retains Coherent. It then runs two investigations through the API and worker passes (`tests.harness.Atlas`) with scripted role answers to answered cards. Playwright gets `ATLAS_E2E_WORKBENCH_URL`. The first database and its specs are unchanged.
- **Files:** `backend/atlas/db/migrations/versions/0036_investigation_follow_up.py` (new), `backend/atlas/investigations/{service,tasks,model,__init__}.py`, `backend/atlas/api/investigations.py`, `tests/integration/test_investigation_follow_up.py` (new), `tests/integration/test_migrations.py` (head `0036`), `tests/fixtures/investigations/lumentum-follow-up.txt` (new; a hand-written note labelled synthetic, imported for Lumentum so round 2 has new Evidence), `frontend/app/{workbench,investigation}/page.tsx` (new), `frontend/lib/workbench.ts` (new), `frontend/lib/{use-api,routes}.ts`, `frontend/lib/api/{client.ts,openapi.json,schema.ts}`, `frontend/app/{layout.tsx,globals.css}`, `frontend/unit/workbench.test.ts` (new), `frontend/e2e/workbench.spec.ts` (new), `scripts/e2e.py`, `docs/decisions.md`, `AGENTS.md`.
- **Tests:**
  - `test_investigation_follow_up.py`, **6 passed**:
    - a follow-up round on an open question, from launch through the round's run: plan, events, audit, same run, the Scout and extraction questions, only unread documents, the redrafted card citing both rounds, `card_before`, the Evidence tray, and round 2's own review job; then a second follow-up is refused;
    - with nothing new to read, the round stops `no_new_independent_evidence` without an Editor call, and the card stands;
    - a premise disproven during round 2 cancels only its Investigator;
    - refusals: 404, `max_rounds: 1`, an unknown or blank question, running, saved as a Hypothesis (with `request.hypothesis_id`);
    - a budget-exhausted investigation is resumed, not followed up;
    - the list, newest first, and its paging.
  - Regression: `test_investigations.py`, `test_hypotheses.py`, `test_migrations.py` and `test_queue_pause.py`, **43 passed**. `tests/unit`: **440 passed**.
  - Frontend: `npm run test` **20 passed** (3 new in `unit/workbench.test.ts`); lint, typecheck and build are clean. `uv run python scripts/e2e.py` with `PLAYWRIGHT_HOST_PLATFORM_OVERRIDE=ubuntu22.04-x64`: **9 passed**, including the 2 new workbench specs. The first opens an investigation, checks the plan's statuses, the Evidence tray quote and span link, "no counterevidence", the event stream and the open questions; launches the follow-up; sees round 2 queued, `follow_up_started` and the follow-up blocked; then opens the span highlighted in the viewer. The second saves an investigation as a Hypothesis. Both use only auto-retrying assertions.

## 2026-09-30: Phase 3-6a ticket 31, TradingView as an owner-override source (off by default)

- **Built:**
  - `atlas.tradingview`: `mcp.py`, an MCP client over streamable HTTP (MCP 2025-06-18). It sends `initialize`, then `notifications/initialized`, then `tools/call`, each request carrying `Mcp-Session-Id` and `MCP-Protocol-Version`. It reads JSON or SSE answers, re-initializes once after a 404 (ended session), closes with DELETE, and raises `TradingViewUnavailable` on 429/5xx/transport errors and `McpToolError` on `isError`. Every request waits for a process-wide token bucket (`ATLAS_TRADINGVIEW_RATE_PER_S`, default 0.5, at most 1). `client.py` covers the three tools only (documents, document view, news), with typed models, at most `ATLAS_TRADINGVIEW_MAX_CALLS_PER_JOB` calls, and each call recorded. `tokens.py` loads the token file or secrets, refreshes before expiry and once after a 401, writes rotated tokens back (0600, atomic) and never logs a token. `login.py` is `atlas tradingview login`: protected-resource metadata (from the 401 challenge, else the well-known URIs), authorization-server metadata, dynamic client registration, then code + PKCE S256 with `state` and RFC 8707 `resource`, on a loopback redirect. `service.py` holds the `tradingview_catalog` and `tradingview_transcripts` jobs; `reads.py` the catalog read.
  - Ledger: `SourceCandidate.transcript` (`TradingViewTranscript`) becomes a Tier B Source Document (source type `transcript`, publisher `<upstream> via TradingView`, licence `licensed:tradingview-owner-override`), with `available_at` = reported (basis `publisher_timestamp`). `metadata.tradingview` and `metadata.owner_override` are stored on the version, and `SourceLedger.record(owner_override=)` fills `fetch_observation.owner_override`.
  - Parser: `application/x-tradingview-transcript+json` → `tradingview-transcript-v1`, which flattens the AST to one line per paragraph with speaker labels and treats `br` as a line break. Other parse paths are unchanged (still `text-v2`).
  - Leads: `lead.origin` (`searxng` | `tradingview_news`), with `first_query_id` nullable for headlines. `tradingview_headline` holds publisher, publisher link, published time, related symbols, company themes and the override. `Lead` gains `origin` and `headline`; `query` and `theme` are now nullable.
  - Budget: a third provider `tradingview` (unit `requests`, `ATLAS_TRADINGVIEW_BUDGET_REQUESTS`, 200 per window), swept from `tradingview_request`.
  - Migration `0035` (down_revision `0033`): `tradingview_request`, `tradingview_catalog_entry`, `tradingview_headline`, `lead.origin`, `fetch_observation.owner_override`, and `provider_usage` accepting `tradingview`.
  - API: `GET /api/v1/tradingview/catalog?company_id=&category=&has_transcript=` (each transcript view with its Source Version). The API client was regenerated.
  - CLI: `atlas tradingview login [--print-tokens] [--port] [--client-id] [--timeout]` and `atlas tradingview check --symbol` (one catalog call, counts only). Both exit 1 while the source is off.
  - Config: `tradingview_symbol` for all 12 companies (universe version 6). Settings `ATLAS_TRADINGVIEW_*` (`.env.example`); `.atlas/` is gitignored. Docs: `docs/decisions.md` (the implementation under the owner-override entry), `docs/runbooks.md` "TradingView (owner override, ticket 31)", AGENTS.md.
- **Files:** `backend/atlas/tradingview/{__init__,model,tokens,login,mcp,client,service,reads,handlers}.py` (new), `backend/atlas/api/tradingview.py` (new), `backend/atlas/db/migrations/versions/0035_tradingview.py` (new), `backend/atlas/{parsing,settings,companies,cli}.py`, `backend/atlas/sources/{adapter,__init__}.py`, `backend/atlas/ledger/{service,reads}.py`, `backend/atlas/discovery/leads.py`, `backend/atlas/jobs/{budget,handlers}.py`, `backend/atlas/api/app.py`, `configs/themes/ai-infrastructure.yaml`, `tests/fakes/tradingview.py` (new), `tests/fixtures/tradingview/` (new, synthetic, with a manifest), `tests/unit/test_tradingview_mcp.py`, `tests/integration/test_tradingview.py`, `tests/integration/test_tradingview_login.py` (new), `tests/unit/test_cli.py`, `tests/integration/test_migrations.py`, `frontend/lib/api/`, `.env.example`, `.gitignore`, docs.
- **Tests (transport-level fake MCP + OAuth server; worker-pass and CLI seams):** `test_tradingview_mcp.py` **13 passed**: the handshake and headers, SSE, news, a 401 → refresh → rotated file (0600, no token in logs), proactive refresh, no-refresh and rejected-refresh errors, no token (nothing sent), a session ended then reopened, tool error vs outage, the per-job bound and recording, pacing ≥ 1 s apart, and the transcript parse. `test_tradingview.py` **6 passed**: off by default (no request); catalog → transcripts → retain, with catalog metadata, a Tier B version, override marks and headline leads; a rerun adds nothing; the per-job bound; the budget holds the transcripts job; a non-retrievable view fails the attempt and is listed. `test_tradingview_login.py` **5 passed**: discovery + DCR + PKCE + token file (0600, nothing printed), the authorization URL's parameters, `--print-tokens`, `check` printing counts only, and both commands refused while off. Regression run: `tests/unit` plus `test_discovery`, `test_quota_pacing`, `test_migrations`, `test_candidates`, `test_manual_import`, `test_queue_pause`, `test_tradingview`, `test_theme_explorer`: **523 passed**. ruff format/check and strict pyright are clean; frontend typecheck and lint are clean.
- **Fixture-tested only:** everything. No call reached TradingView (no token was available to the agent). The MCP transport follows the MCP 2025-06-18 spec, and the tool answer shapes come from the owner's live session (docs/decisions.md). Whether TradingView's authorization server offers dynamic registration, which scopes it wants, and whether it rotates refresh tokens are not verified. `atlas tradingview check` is the first live verification step.
- **Deviations:**
  - The site register and fetch gate don't apply: this is an authenticated API under the owner's account, and the override (`ATLAS_TRADINGVIEW_ENABLED`) is its gate.
  - `available_at` is the event's `reported` time, as the ticket says, although the view's `published` time (kept in metadata) may be later.
  - Only tool calls count toward the budget. Handshake requests are paced but not counted, and `check`'s single call isn't recorded.
  - Headline leads don't enter `propose_candidates` or investigations, which read a discovery's sightings. `/api/v1/leads` and the theme filter show them.
  - `Lead.query` and `Lead.theme` are now nullable in the API.
  - The token file defaults to `.atlas/tradingview-token.json`. Its presence takes precedence over tokens given as secrets.
- **Next:** the owner runs `atlas tradingview login` and `check --symbol NASDAQ:LITE` live, then one `tradingview_catalog` for Lumentum. Feed headline leads to the mention extractor. The owner could surface the catalog in the Company dossier.

## 2026-09-30: integrating tickets 17 and 25 with ticket 19 (the Skeptic ∥ Financial Analyst plan)

- **Why:** tickets 17 and 25 were built on a base without ticket 19, so their scripted LLM answers still followed the old plan (Skeptic only, answers in one ordered queue). With the Financial Analyst in the plan and its job running beside the Skeptic's, in either order, `test_investigation_follow_up.py` errored (the Analyst took the Editor's answer: `KeyError: 'claim_id'`), `test_evaluations.py` failed on EV-CON-001 (the stub had no Financial Analyst answer), and `scripts/e2e.py`'s workbench seeding had the same ordering problem.
- **Changes:**
  - `tests/integration/test_investigation_follow_up.py`: the Skeptic and the Analyst are scripted by role (`script_role`), as in `test_investigations.py`. Their calls are compared in plan order, and the Analyst's call and tokens are part of each round. Round 2's Analyst slot is now asserted `pending`. A follow-up with nothing new still calls the Skeptic and the Analyst, because both read the investigation's accepted Claims across rounds. Only the Editor is skipped.
  - `backend/atlas/evaluation/stubs.py`: an unscripted `financial_analyst` call gets `{"scenarios": []}` (`UNSCRIPTED_DEFAULTS`). No metric scores scenarios, so this works the way the SearXNG stub's empty results do. Every other unscripted call still fails the case. `gold.py` accepts `financial_analyst` as a script role, and `docs/evaluation-methodology.md` says so. The committed cases are unchanged: they're immutable, and a fix would have to be a new, superseding case. EV-CON-001 again passes on the Skeptic's contradiction (`contradiction_discovery`).
  - `scripts/e2e.py`: the Skeptic and the Analyst are scripted by role.

Regression run: `test_investigations`, `test_hypotheses`, `test_scenarios`, `test_retention_triage`, `test_tradingview`, `test_tradingview_login` and `tests/unit` **539 passed**. ruff (CI paths) and strict pyright are clean, and `scripts/gen_api_client.sh --check` passes. `scripts/e2e.py` with `PLAYWRIGHT_HOST_PLATFORM_OVERRIDE=ubuntu22.04-x64`: **9 passed**.

## 2026-09-30: Phase 3-6a ticket 20, the publish gate and Research Snapshots

- **Built:**
  - **The publish gate** (`backend/atlas/hypotheses/service.py`) gains two default checks. `relationship_not_approved`: every Relationship a version's findings' Assertions support must be owner-approved; machine review is not approval, and rejected never is. `relationship_review_pending`: a finding's Assertion that hasn't been machine-reviewed yet holds publication. `GateFailure` carries `relationship_ids` or `assertion_ids`. The publish endpoint's 422 `publish_gate_failed` now includes typed `failures: [{code, message, relationship_ids, assertion_ids}]` (`PublishRefusalEnvelope` in the OpenAPI schema).
  - **Research Snapshots** (`backend/atlas/snapshots.py`): a publish hook that the API always installs builds the snapshot in the publishing transaction. It puts the canonical JSON in the archive's new `snapshots` namespace (`archive://snapshots/sha256/<hex>`), inserts the `research_snapshot` row and audits `research_snapshot.created` with the hash. Contents are listed in `docs/decisions.md`, "The publish gate and Research Snapshots". `GET /api/v1/snapshots[?hypothesis_id=]` lists the rows. `GET /api/v1/snapshots/{id}` re-hashes the archived bytes against the row on every read; an altered or missing object is 500 `snapshot_integrity_failed`.
  - **Migration `0038_research_snapshot`** (down_revision `0032`; the lead re-chains): the insert-only table, with triggers refusing UPDATE, DELETE, TRUNCATE and an INSERT for an unpublished version, and a CHECK tying the URI to the hash.
  - **Metric** `atlas_research_snapshots_total`.
- **Files:**
  - `backend/atlas/snapshots.py` (new), `backend/atlas/api/snapshots.py` (new), `backend/atlas/db/migrations/versions/0038_research_snapshot.py` (new)
  - `backend/atlas/hypotheses/service.py`, `backend/atlas/api/hypotheses.py` (the router now takes the archive)
  - shared files: `backend/atlas/api/app.py` (two router lines), `backend/atlas/archive/__init__.py` (the `SNAPSHOTS` namespace and URI regex), `backend/atlas/metrics.py`
  - `frontend/lib/api/{openapi.json,schema.ts}` (regenerated)
  - `tests/integration/test_hypotheses.py`, `tests/integration/test_migrations.py` (head `0038`)
  - docs: `docs/decisions.md`, `docs/data-model.md` §3.1i, `AGENTS.md`; the ticket
- **Tests** (HTTP API seam, real Postgres, filesystem archive; recorded Hindsight fake, scripted LiteLLM and SearXNG fakes):
  - `test_the_publish_gate_needs_a_falsifier_an_unresolved_question_and_approved_relationships` (extends ticket 16's gate test). Publishing is refused with `no_falsifier`, `no_unresolved_question` and `relationship_not_approved` naming the machine-reviewed edge, and no snapshot is written. After a correction, a rejected edge is still refused. The owner then approves the edge, the publication succeeds, and it has one snapshot of version 2.
  - `test_publishing_freezes_a_research_snapshot_of_what_the_version_was_built_from`: the refreshed Bottlenecks gaps, the Skeptic's 10-Q counterevidence, and a researcher scenario with an XBRL-sourced input. The test recomputes the canonical JSON's SHA-256 and compares it with the row, the URI and the archived file. It checks:
    - the cutoff, and the Source Versions considered (10-K, 10-Q, companyfacts) with the ledger's hashes and `available_at`;
    - Memory as returned (the Scout's gaps, exactly) and the recall selections;
    - both Assertions' spans against the archived parse, and the approved Relationship;
    - the scenario's hashes and the dataset hash;
    - the role calls' prompt versions and hashes and the routed models (against `GET /runs/{id}/role-calls`), the run kinds and Hindsight version 0.10.1;
    - the outputs, the audit event with the hash, and the metric.
  - `test_a_published_snapshot_can_t_be_altered`: UPDATE, DELETE and TRUNCATE are refused by the database, and so is a snapshot row for an unpublished version. An archived object altered in place is 500 `snapshot_integrity_failed` without the altered content, a deleted one is too ("missing"), and the restored bytes verify again.
  - The lifecycle and correction tests now approve the Relationships before publishing.
  - Results: `test_hypotheses.py` 11 passed. With `test_migrations.py` and `test_archive_contract.py`: 55 passed. Earlier, with `test_scenarios.py` and `tests/unit`: 523 passed. ruff format/check (backend, tests, scripts), strict pyright, `gen_api_client.sh --check` and the frontend typecheck are clean. The full suite runs on the owner's runners.
  - The gate tests were written with the code, not run red first. The snapshot contents were first dumped from a throwaway test to write concrete expectations.
- **Fixture-only vs live:** everything. No live LLM, Hindsight or SearXNG call was made. The Hindsight version in the snapshot is the recorded fake's.
- **Deviations:**
  - The archive key is `snapshots/sha256/<hex>` (URI `archive://snapshots/sha256/<hex>`), not map ticket 09's `snapshots/<sha256>`, so the archive keeps one key scheme.
  - Atlas stores no recall results. The Investigator's recall only chose Source Version sections, and the model was sent their text. So the snapshot lists those choices (`recall_selections`) rather than memory text. Memory text that did reach a role (a mental model's content in `retrieved`) is recorded exactly.
  - An integrity failure is a 500 (`snapshot_integrity_failed`): the server's stored data is wrong, not the request.
  - `relationship_review_pending` has no integration test. The investigation's final stop always chains the machine review, so the seams can't produce an unreviewed finding Assertion without reaching into internals.
  - The snapshot's `put` happens inside the DB transaction. If the transaction later rolls back, an orphaned content-addressed object stays in the archive (harmless).
- **Next:** the replay (map ticket 09's local `atlas-replay-<id>` bank seeded from Source Versions with `available_at ≤ cutoff`) can read the snapshot's `source_versions` and `cutoff`. The Hypothesis dossier page (ticket 24) can link a published version's snapshot.
Migration renumbering: ticket 20's 0038 → 0036 (after 0035 tradingview).

## 2026-09-30: fix, XBRL normalization failed on restatement links (live verification)

- **Bug:** live ingests of Coherent, AXT and Applied Optoelectronics failed with `financial_observation_previous_observation_id_fkey`. **Root cause:** `normalize` linked each new observation to its predecessor in filing order but returned them in companyfacts' document order, and the service inserted them in that order. SEC lists some keys' facts newest filing first (Coherent's `AccruedBonusesCurrent` at 2010-06-30: the 2011-08 10-K, then the 2011-05, 2011-02 and 2010-11 10-Qs), so a row's `previous_observation_id` named a row not yet inserted. Lumentum's facts happened to be oldest first. On the full live Coherent document (27,492 facts), 97 new observations linked forward.
- **Fix:** `normalize` returns new observations in filing order (`Observation.order`), so each predecessor precedes its successor; the service checks every link names a stored or earlier row before inserting (`NormalizationLinkError`) and re-checks its version's record after taking the filer's lock. The foreign key stays. A normalization failure no longer fails the ingest: it is rolled back, recorded in the insert-only, audited `financial_normalization_failure` (migration `0037`), named in the job's artifacts (`financial_normalization.status: failed`), counted in `atlas_financial_normalizations_total{outcome}` and alerted on (`AtlasFinancialNormalizationFailing`); the next ingest tries again. A database outage still fails the attempt. Rules in `docs/decisions.md`, "XBRL normalization".
- **Files:** `backend/atlas/financials/{xbrl,service,__init__}.py`, `backend/atlas/ledger/ingest.py`, `backend/atlas/metrics.py`, `backend/atlas/db/migrations/versions/0037_financial_normalization_failure.py` (new; down_revision `0036`, the lead re-chains), `configs/prometheus/atlas-alerts.yaml`; tests `tests/integration/test_financials.py`, `tests/integration/test_migrations.py` (head `0037`), `tests/unit/test_alert_rules.py`; fixtures `tests/fixtures/financials/edgar/coherent/` (companyfacts recorded from SEC once on 2026-09-30 and trimmed to three keys listed newest first; hand-written empty submissions) and `tests/fixtures/financials/edgar/schema-drift/` (hand-written, a fact without `val`), both companies added to `tests/fixtures/financials/themes.yaml`; docs `docs/decisions.md`, `AGENTS.md`.
- **Tests** (worker single pass + HTTP API, real Postgres):
  - `test_facts_listed_newest_filing_first_link_in_filing_order`: red first with the live error (`ForeignKeyViolation ... financial_observation_previous_observation_id_fkey`), then green. The ingest succeeds, and the histories of the three keys are chained in filing order: a reaffirm chain, a later restatement, and a sign-change restatement flagged `sign_change` and `large_change`.
  - `test_a_failed_normalization_is_recorded_and_the_ingest_succeeds`: two ingests of the drifted companyfacts both succeed. Each records a failure row, an audit event and a `failed` artifact, and stores no observation. The metric reads failed 2 and succeeded 0, and `atlas audit verify` passes.
  - Results: `test_financials.py`, `test_xbrl_normalization.py`, `test_alert_rules.py`, `test_ingest.py`, `test_migrations.py`, `test_queue_pause.py`: **63 passed**. `ruff format --check` and `ruff check` (backend tests scripts) and `pyright` are clean.
- **Live vs fixture:** the tests use fixtures only. One SEC request fetched Coherent's companyfacts to find the pattern. A throwaway local test, not committed, then replayed that full document through the ingest twice: the first run normalized 27,492 observations and the second did nothing. AXT and Applied Optoelectronics were not re-run live.
- **Next:** re-run the live verification ingests for Coherent, AXT and Applied Optoelectronics.

## 2026-09-30: Phase 3-6a ticket 21, contradiction proposes an update

- **Built:**
  - **Dependencies** (`backend/atlas/proposed_updates/triggers.py`): `Hypotheses.publish` records, in the publishing transaction, what the version depends on: its findings' Assertions, the Claims they cite, the Relationships those Assertions support and the spans' Source Versions (`hypothesis_dependency`, insert-only).
  - **Event hooks**, each in the transaction that records its event. Each runs one indexed query and enqueues the deterministic `check_contradictions` job only when a published version is touched:
    - `Assertions.review`: a dependent Assertion disputed, rejected or superseded;
    - `Relationships.review`: the owner rejects a dependent edge;
    - `SourceLedger.record`: a new Source Version of a document whose earlier version is cited;
    - the Skeptic's `_insert`: accepted, independent counterevidence against a Claim stating a dependent span.
  - **The job** (`detection.py`, not pausable: no LLM) works out the contradicting Evidence. For a revision that is "the new parsed text no longer contains a cited quote"; a revision that still states it, or has no parsed text, proposes nothing. The job flags each Hypothesis's latest published version with one `proposed_update` per event (unique per version and event key). The update lists the Evidence, the affected findings and the open Candidates it concerns, and is audited `proposed_update.created` by `atlas-contradictions`.
  - **API** (`backend/atlas/api/proposed_updates.py`):
    - `GET /api/v1/proposed-updates[/{id}]?hypothesis_id=&candidate_id=&state=` and `GET /api/v1/hypotheses/{id}/proposed-updates`;
    - `Hypothesis.open_proposed_updates`;
    - `POST .../accept` (`{note?}`): a new draft correction from the latest version, its affected findings `needs_review` with the summary as a limitation, audited `hypothesis.corrected` + `proposed_update.accepted`;
    - `POST .../dismiss` (`{reason}`), audited.
  - **Migration `0039_proposed_updates`** (down_revision `0038`): both tables. It backfills the dependencies of versions already published. A trigger lets a proposed update be resolved once and never deleted.
- **Files:**
  - new: `backend/atlas/proposed_updates/{__init__,triggers,detection,model,service}.py`, `backend/atlas/api/proposed_updates.py`, `backend/atlas/db/migrations/versions/0039_proposed_updates.py`, `tests/integration/test_proposed_updates.py`
  - hooks (a few lines each): `backend/atlas/hypotheses/service.py`, `backend/atlas/assertions.py`, `backend/atlas/relationships/service.py`, `backend/atlas/ledger/service.py`, `backend/atlas/investigations/skeptic.py`
  - `backend/atlas/hypotheses/model.py` (`open_proposed_updates`)
  - shared files: `backend/atlas/api/app.py` (one router), `backend/atlas/jobs/handlers.py` (one registration)
  - `frontend/lib/api/{openapi.json,schema.ts}` (regenerated)
  - `tests/integration/test_migrations.py` (head `0039`)
  - docs: `docs/decisions.md` ("Proposed updates"), `docs/data-model.md` §3.1j, `CONTEXT.md` (Proposed Update), `AGENTS.md`; the ticket
- **Tests** (HTTP API, CLI and worker-pass seams, real Postgres, editable copy of the recorded EDGAR fixtures; recorded Hindsight fake, scripted LiteLLM and SearXNG fakes):
  - The gate test, `test_a_later_contradictory_source_proposes_one_update_and_leaves_the_snapshot_untouched`:
    - A Hypothesis is published on the Coherent 10-K's supply quote.
    - Collection re-runs on a revision that keeps the quote: no update.
    - A revision that drops "strategic multi-year supply agreement" gives **exactly one** `source_revised` update. It names the new and old Source Versions, the withdrawn Assertion, the finding, and a Candidate for Coherent (inserted in the DB as setup).
    - The version row, the snapshot row, the archived snapshot bytes (so the hash) and the verified snapshot read are identical before and after.
    - A further ingest and a hand-re-enqueued job add nothing.
    - Checked red: with the source hook disabled, the test fails.
  - `test_the_owner_accepts_an_update_as_a_correction_or_dismisses_it_with_a_reason`:
    - A later document is imported by hand (`atlas sources import`). The owner's Assertion on it supersedes the cited one, and the owner rejects the dependent edge: two updates.
    - Dismiss needs a reason (422 without one) and resolves once (409 after).
    - Accept creates draft version 2 (correction, based on 1, finding `needs_review` with the limitation).
    - Checked: the audit trail; that version 2 is refused by the gate while its edge is rejected; that the DB refuses re-opening or deleting an update; and that the snapshot is unchanged.
  - `test_later_independent_counterevidence_against_a_cited_statement_proposes_an_update`: a second investigation's Skeptic quotes the 10-Q against the same 10-K span. One `counterevidence` update bears on the published version's own Assertion.
  - Results: `test_proposed_updates.py` **3 passed**. `test_hypotheses`, `test_migrations`, `test_assertions`, `test_relationships`, `test_queue_pause`, `test_jobs` and `tests/unit`: **560 passed**. `test_investigations`, `test_ingest`, `test_manual_import`, `test_evidence_families` and `test_retention`: **69 passed**. ruff format/check (backend, tests, scripts), strict pyright, `gen_api_client.sh --check` and the frontend typecheck are clean. The full suite runs on the owner's runners.
- **Fixture-only vs live:** everything. No live LLM, Hindsight, SearXNG or SEC call was made.
- **Deviations:**
  - "Contradiction" is deterministic, so a new, separate document contradicts a version only through an Assertion review (supersede/dispute) or the Skeptic's counterevidence. A revision of a cited document contradicts only when a cited quote disappears.
  - The Candidate flag is the update's `candidate_ids` and `GET /proposed-updates?candidate_id=`. The Candidate read model is unchanged. The test's Candidate is inserted directly, because committing one for an already-seeded company isn't possible through the API.
  - Only the latest published version of each Hypothesis is flagged. No metric was added.
- **Next:** the Hypothesis dossier page (ticket 24) can show open proposed updates with accept/dismiss. A metric `atlas_proposed_updates{state}` if the owner wants one.

## 2026-09-30: Phase 3-6a ticket 24, the Hypothesis dossier page (E)

- **Built:**
  - **`/hypotheses/`**: the Hypotheses list (thesis of the latest version, status, theme, versions, which are published, updated), linked from the site navigation.
  - **`/hypothesis/?id=&version=`**: the dossier of one version (default the latest), with a version selector that puts the version in the URL. Sections:
    - the Hypothesis (status, theme, author, first publication, next review, investigation) and JSON/Markdown export links for the chosen version (`/export?format=json|markdown&version=`);
    - the version's record (origin, note, author, publication, content SHA-256), thesis, mechanism, predictions, catalysts, falsifiers, required evidence, alternative explanations, unresolved questions;
    - findings from the JSON export, each citation `[n]` opening its span highlighted on the version page (`/version/?id=&assertion=`), a citations table (quote, source, the Assertion's review state now and when written), needs-review, limitations, open questions, counterevidence anchors; the Editor's unsupported statements labeled "Unsupported (not promoted)";
    - the Skeptic's contradictions, each with its quote and span link;
    - the diff between two versions (from/to selectors; default the chosen version and the one before), claims grouped new / contradicted / unchanged / removed with counts, and the other fields changed;
    - the version's scenarios: inputs (low/base/high; sourced from an XBRL observation with a link to its Source Version, or from an Assertion with its quote and span link; estimated with its basis; missing), outputs per case (blocked lines say which inputs they need), and the ±20% sensitivity table; the hashes and whether recomputation is byte-identical;
    - lifecycle and publication: buttons for the allowed transitions, the publish gate for the latest version with each failed check explained, `relationship_not_approved` listing each edge by name and linking to `/relationship/?id=`, `relationship_review_pending` linking each Assertion's span; "Publish version N". A refusal re-reads the gate;
    - Research Snapshots: per snapshot, the integrity check (`GET /snapshots/{id}` verifies on read; a 500 `snapshot_integrity_failed` is shown as a failed check), SHA-256, archive URI and size, cutoff, who froze it, and a contents summary (Source Versions, Assertions, Relationships, scenarios, XBRL observations, runs, role calls, whether Memory reached the run, the question);
    - history: the status changes.

The page shows what publishing needs before the owner clicks.

Coherent is ingested from the EDGAR fixtures and an investigation runs (the Investigator quotes the supply agreement and NVIDIA's investment; the Skeptic quotes the 10-Q's share count). The Hypothesis is saved and drafted with the supply finding. A researcher scenario is attached (FY2026 revenue from XBRL, Coherent's share sourced from the supply Assertion, the rest estimated). Then the Hypothesis is moved to evidence-ready, the supply edge is approved, version 1 is published, and a correction (version 2) adds the investment finding, whose `owns` edge is left unapproved. `scripts/e2e.py` serves a second API on it (`ATLAS_E2E_HYPOTHESIS_BASE_URL`).
