Frontend `lint` and `typecheck` clean, `test` 42 passed. `ruff format --check`, `ruff check` (backend, tests, scripts) and strict `pyright` (0 errors): clean. API client regenerated with the script's `client` command.
- **Not run:** the e2e (`scripts/e2e.py`, `scripts/e2e_hypothesis.py` edited by hand), so the fallback card's rendering on the investigation page is checked by lint and typecheck only, not in a browser; the frontend build; the full integration suite; `scripts/ci.sh`; the live suites and their rehearsals (`tests/live/verify.py` edited, not run).
- **Fixture-only vs live:** nothing live. The cut-off answers are scripted (the fake's `finish_reason` and token counts); whether MiniMax reports `finish_reason: length` through LiteLLM is assumed from the OpenAI completion shape LiteLLM returns, with the token-count rule as the fallback; whether MiniMax cites `ref`s faithfully is unmeasured.
- **Deviations and readings:**
  - **Migration needed:** `role_call.status` has a CHECK listing the statuses, so revision `0061` as the ticket says.
  - **The first Editor cap is 8,192** (the role's cap), so one doubling reaches the 16,384 bound; the ticket did not fix the first cap.
  - **A valid answer at the cap is used**, not recorded truncated: only an answer that fails validation at the cap is truncated.
  - **A quarantined Editor now gets the fallback card at once** instead of failing the job attempt (before: retried up to 3 attempts, then no card). Other non-transient Editor failures (an HTTP 4xx) still fail the attempt and, after the job's attempts, end without a card, as before.
  - **The fallback with no accepted Claim stops `no_new_independent_evidence`**, as an Editor-written card with no Claim does.

- **Built, in the ticket's order** (one commit; each part tested): (1) the `cancelled` retain state, its class, the metric and the memory read showing it apart, `retry-failed` enqueueing it, and migration `0061` moving the recorded cancellations; (2) the per-document outcome after any operation end; (3) the transient class, bounded by `ATLAS_RETAIN_TRANSIENT_RETRIES` (default 3); (4) partial extraction from `extraction_errors_count`, retried once; (5) `retry-failed --error-class … --company …`, backfill class by default. Rules: `docs/decisions.md`, "A section's outcome comes from its own document".

Changed: the memory read's `counts` now has `cancelled`; the failed-operation test checks the class `permanent`.
  - The quota acceptance ("still pauses as today") is the existing `test_a_429_failed_operation_pauses_the_queue_then_resumes_and_resubmits`, unchanged and passing.
  - **No red run:** the tests were written with the code, not before it; no failing run of them was seen.
  - **Results:** `tests/integration/test_retention.py`, `test_migrations.py`, `tests/unit/test_hindsight_fake_derivations.py`: 47 passed. `test_queue_pause.py`, `test_quota_pacing.py`, `test_replay.py`: 41 passed. `tests/unit`: 841 passed. `ruff format --check`, `ruff check` (backend, tests, scripts) and `pyright`: clean. Frontend `lint`, `typecheck`, `test` (42 passed). One audit-action rename in `service.py` (`memory_document.extraction_errors`) was made after those runs; ruff and pyright were run again after it (clean), and the three new `test_retention.py` tests on the settlement path (`-k "extraction_errors or own_document or cancelled"`: 3 passed), not the whole modules.

- **Fixture-only vs live:** nothing live. Hindsight is the recorded fake with two new derivations (listed in `docs/decisions.md` beside the earlier ones): a batch held `failed`/`cancelled` stores only the documents the test names (the others answer 404 with a hand-written body), and `report_extraction_errors` sets the recorded final status's `extraction_errors_count`. Not checked against Hindsight 0.10.2: whether a failed or cancelled batch stores any documents, what a cancelled operation's message is, and what `extraction_errors_count` counts.
- **Readings of the ticket and deviations:**
  - **A transient failure pauses the queue** (as an outage, class `unavailable` on the pause; the operation keeps `transient`): the queue has no per-job delay, so "resubmitted with the queue's backoff" is read as its pause. The failure that passes the bound does not pause.
  - **The fake's held failures now store nothing by default** (before, a held failed batch's documents existed with facts). Every existing test that holds a failed retain passed unchanged; a document an earlier batch stored stays stored when a later batch fails.
  - **Partial is not a retain state**: a partial section is `completed` (its facts are in memory) with `extraction_errors > 0`, shown as `partial` on the memory read and counted by `atlas_partial_sections`. The retry uses the reprocess's once-only counter, so a section reprocessed for zero facts is not retried again for errors. The count is the operation's, so all its sections are retried.
  - **`retry-failed` enqueues backfill class by default** (`--interactive` to opt out; `--backfill` still accepted); it resets the class and the transient count, and its summary adds `by_error_class`, `job_class`, `error_classes` and `company`.
  - **The other classes of rows recorded before `0061`**: `missing` for "document … not found in Hindsight after its operation completed", else `permanent`; every cancelled operation is classed `cancelled`. The migration's moves are not audited (no audit row can be written in SQL); each row keeps its error text.
  - **The backfill** takes cancelled sections through `retry-failed` (spec ticket 12); `atlas ingest --backfill` still skips versions that have memory documents.
  - **Long Items:** the error grouping the ticket cites shows only cancellations, no section failing by size, so nothing is split.
- **For ticket 02 (the health read), what it must add:** sections by retain state including `cancelled`; failed and cancelled sections by `memory_document.error_class` (`cancelled`, `permanent`, `transient`, `missing`); partial sections (`retain_state = 'completed' AND extraction_errors > 0`); operations by `error_class`, now also `transient` and `cancelled`.
- **Next:** the lead re-chains `0061` onto main's head and runs CI. On production after the deploy: `atlas_retained_sections_total{outcome="cancelled"}` should read about 1,907 and `failed` near 0; the backlog is re-enqueued a company at a time by `retry-failed --error-class cancelled --company <slug>` once the retain profile (tickets 04–06) is in.

## 2026-10-02: release 0.3.1 (the Editor's card); memory-quality tickets 01 to 03 integrated

- **Why 0.3.1:** pilot investigation 1 on 0.3.0 (`fcb1ef32-50fd-4788-84c3-e2397a92dd97`) ended with no research card: with six Investigators and 89 accepted Claims, the Editor's answer was cut off at its 4,096-token output cap on all six attempts. A blocker under the pilot-review map's focus rule, so ticket 16 is released alone.
- **The lead's check of ticket 16 before the release:**
  - the diff read against the ticket; `ruff format --check`, `ruff check` and strict `pyright` clean on the merged tree; the implementer's test modules re-run in its worktree (`test_role_calls`, `test_investigations`, `test_investigation_follow_up`, `test_migrations`, `test_bottleneck_prompts`): 90 passed;
  - **a live replay of the failed request** (`.scratch/tools/editor_replay.py`; one MiniMax-M3 chat completion through LiteLLM, the request rebuilt from production's saved role call with the merged code's `editor.v6`): 89 Claims by short reference, 34,615 tokens in, **3,181 out against the 8,192 cap**, 40 seconds, `finish_reason` `stop`; the answer validates: 8 findings citing 79 distinct references, none unknown, verdict `needs_review`. So MiniMax cites the references faithfully on this request and the card fits with room. The retry at a larger cap and the fallback card are tested on the scripted fake only.
- **Runners:** `integrate/mq-16` at `a6d28ae`, run 36985943450: **1,409 passed, 17 deselected, the frontend gates (42 unit tests), the API client check and the e2e (10 passed)**. That run covers the e2e seeds the implementer edited by hand. `main` fast-forwarded to `a6d28ae`; tag `v0.3.1` there; release run 36990029189.
- **Release:** run 36990029189 succeeded (`ci` 26m31s on the runners, `publish` 1m26s): `ghcr.io/ekenheim/atlas:0.3.1`. Main's own run 36990023143 succeeded too. **Deploy:** home-ops PR #7195 (the image, and `ATLAS_LLM_ROLE_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` 360 so a 16,384-token Editor answer cannot time out at the default 180 s); the owner merges.
- **One acceptance box of ticket 16 stays open:** no browser test renders the fallback card on the investigation page.
- **Memory-quality tickets 01, 02 and 03 are merged on the integration branch** (not in 0.3.1): 01 Hindsight 0.10.2 recorded (`18cd1f0`), 02 the memory-health read and the probe set (`0c921b6`), 03 a section's outcome (`b45b914`).
  - **Checked by the lead:** ticket 01's recordings scanned for the LiteLLM key and host (no hit); ticket 02's hand-written listings compared with ticket 01's recorded ones (same shape); each diff read against its ticket.
  - **Integration changes:** ticket 03's migration is `0068` after `0061` (ticket 16 took `0061`); tickets 02 and 03 each added a `report_extraction_errors` derivation to the Hindsight fake, of which ticket 03's `(count, where=, times=)` is kept; the health read shows `cancelled` as a state of its own, groups the sections not in memory by state, error class and normalized error, and reads `partial` from `memory_document.extraction_errors`, with a new test for the cancelled case; the API client regenerated after each merge.
  - **Local results on the merged tree (WSL):** static checks clean; `test_retention`, `test_migrations`, `test_queue_pause`, `test_quota_pacing`, `test_replay`, `test_memory_health` and all of `tests/unit`: 967 passed. The runners have not run this tree yet.
- **What ticket 01 changed in the plan** (the tickets and the spec are updated): one observation scope per theme and no company scope, because consolidation costs one LLM request per scope touched, on the shared Codex subscription; the backfill deletes a Hindsight document and retains it again, because a retain of unchanged content under the same ID re-extracts nothing; chunk-exact pointers are built, because a chunk's text is a verbatim slice of the retained content.
- **The before-measure** of recall (91 probes, production 0.3.0) is in pilot-review ticket 20.

## 2026-10-02: memory-quality ticket 06, observation scopes (one per theme)

- **Built:** every retain item now sends explicit `observation_scopes`, one per theme tag (`[["theme:photonics"]]` for a company in one theme, one scope per theme for a company in several), no company scope; the company, source, doctype and form tags stay on the item. `atlas.retention.service.observation_scopes(tags)` builds them and `retain_item` sends them, so retention and replay banks (and live evaluation banks, which retain through the same jobs) send the same scopes. An item with no theme tag sends none (Hindsight's default). `RetainItem` gained `observation_scopes: list[list[str]] | None` (excluded when None). No migration, no API change.
- **The fake** (`tests/fakes/hindsight.py`): `derive_observation(..., scope=tags)` is the observation of that explicit scope (tags = the scope's alone, as `observation_scopes/05`–`08` recorded) and refuses a scope a source item didn't send; with `derive_memories` on and no scopes scripted, `GET …/observations/scopes` is derived from `observation_scopes/09-list-scopes`. Listed in `docs/decisions.md` beside the fake's other derivations.
- **Files:** `backend/atlas/hindsight/models.py`, `backend/atlas/retention/service.py`, `tests/fakes/hindsight.py`, `tests/integration/test_observation_scopes.py` (new), `tests/integration/test_replay.py`, `tests/integration/test_retention.py` (the item's field list gains `observation_scopes`; the shape test asserts the scope), `tests/unit/test_hindsight_contract.py` (the two recorded scope retains submitted through the gateway), `docs/decisions.md` ("Observation scopes: one per theme"), `AGENTS.md`, the ticket.

A first run errored on the health test because the read also lists entities (now scripted empty).

## 2026-10-02: memory-quality ticket 07, recall as a reading index

- **Built:**
  - **The gateway's recall** (`HindsightGateway.recall`) takes `max_tokens`, `types`, `prefer_observations`, `query_timestamp` (sent in UTC; a naive time is refused) and `include_source_facts` / `include_chunks`, each sent only when given, so a recall without them is the request it always was. `RecallResult` keeps the answer's `entities` map (canonical name → entity ID), `chunks`, `source_facts` and `source_facts_truncated`; each `Memory` keeps its `scores` (`RecallScores`), `entities` (names; the memory list's comma-joined string is split) and `chunk_id`, and an observation's `source_fact_ids` land in `source_memory_ids`.
  - **The provenance resolver** (`resolve_recalled(memory, answer)`) seeds its memory cache with the answer's source facts, so an observation whose sources are in the answer resolves with no request; a listed source the answer left out (truncation, or a deleted fact) is asked for as before, so the citation states are unchanged.
  - **Pointer recalls** (the Scout's and the Skeptic's, `Tasks._theme_recall`) send `ATLAS_POINTER_RECALL_MAX_TOKENS` (new setting, 8192), budget `high`, `prefer_observations`, the investigation's `as_of` as `query_timestamp` and `include.source_facts`. A replay's recalls send its cutoff as `query_timestamp`.
  - **`POST /api/v1/memory/recall`** accepts `max_tokens`, `types`, `prefer_observations`, `query_timestamp`, `include_source_facts` (all optional) and returns per memory `scores`, `entities` (`name`, `entity_id`) and `chunk_id`, and `source_facts_truncated`.
  - **Reading pointers** store `score` (the recall's `scores.final`) and `entity_names` (migration `0063`, `down_revision = "0061"`; nullable; the downgrade refuses while any pointer carries either); the investigation read (`ReadingPointer`) and the Research Snapshot's `memory.reading_pointers` carry them. Pointed companies are ranked as before.
  - **The fake**: a derived recall asking for source facts gets `source_fact_ids` and a `source_facts` map built from `recall_options/06`; `truncate_source_facts(keep)` cuts it and sets the flag. Listed in `docs/decisions.md` beside the other derivations.
  - `docs/decisions.md`, "Recall as a reading index"; `AGENTS.md` line; API client regenerated.
- **Files:** `backend/atlas/hindsight/{gateway,models,__init__}.py`, `backend/atlas/research/{provenance,service}.py`, `backend/atlas/investigations/{tasks,pointers,model}.py`, `backend/atlas/replay/service.py`, `backend/atlas/snapshots.py`, `backend/atlas/settings.py`, `backend/atlas/db/migrations/versions/0063_pointer_score_and_entities.py` (new), `tests/fakes/hindsight.py`, `tests/integration/test_{research,investigations,replay,hypotheses,migrations}.py`, `frontend/lib/api/{openapi.json,schema.ts}`, `AGENTS.md`, `docs/{decisions,implementation-log}.md`, the ticket.
- **Tests** (WSL, the shared Postgres; actual results):
  - Red run: the five new `test_research.py` tests failed before the code existed (422 `include_source_facts: Extra inputs are not permitted`; the no-new-field test on the missing `source_facts_truncated`).
  - `tests/integration/test_research.py tests/integration/test_replay.py tests/unit`: **927 passed**. New in `test_research.py`: no new field sends the old body; the new fields are sent and scores, entities (with IDs from the answer's map) and chunk IDs come back; an observation resolves from the answer's source facts with no `GET …/memories/<id>`; with the answer truncated to one source fact only the missing one is asked for; a deleted source is still `broken`/`source_memory_not_found` with one request. `test_replay.py` asserts each replay recall's `query_timestamp` is the cutoff.
  - `tests/integration/test_investigations.py test_hypotheses.py test_migrations.py test_investigation_follow_up.py`: **72 passed**. New investigation test: the Scout's three recalls carry exactly budget high, `max_tokens` 8192, `prefer_observations`, the as-of time and the source-facts include; no per-memory request; every pointer's `score` and `entity_names` equal the derived recall's (the recorded result of its type); pointed companies unchanged. The insert-only test still refuses UPDATE/DELETE/TRUNCATE; the snapshot test compares `score` and `entity_names` with the read. Migration head is `0063`.
  - `ruff format --check`, `ruff check`, strict `pyright`: clean. Frontend `lint`, `typecheck`, `test` (42 passed).
- **Not run:** the full suite, e2e, `scripts/ci.sh`; nothing live.
- **Fixture-only vs live:** fixture-only. The fake ignores `max_tokens`, `types`, `prefer_observations` and `query_timestamp` (results as before; scores and entities as recorded), so their effect on what is recalled rests on ticket 01's recordings. The lead's probe set (`scripts/memory_probe.py`) and a pilot investigation show it live.
- **Deviations and readings:** the public API takes `include_source_facts` only (not `include_chunks`; the gateway supports chunks for ticket 08). Pointer recalls send no `types` (all types by default, which `prefer_observations` needs). The resolver asks for any listed source missing from the answer's map, not only when the truncation flag is set, so a deleted source stays `broken` exactly as before. Entity IDs and chunk IDs are not stored on the pointer; they stay in the recall answer for tickets 08 and 09. Migration `0063` will conflict with siblings' heads in `test_migrations.py` (expected).
- **Next:** ticket 08 (chunk-exact pointers) can add `include_chunks=True` to `_theme_recall` and read `RecallResult.chunks`; ticket 09 uses `RecalledMemory.entities[].entity_id`; the pilot review judges `score` before anything is weighted by it.

## 2026-10-02: memory-quality ticket 19, Atlas decides when Memory consolidates

- **Built** (`docs/decisions.md`, "Atlas decides when Memory consolidates"): bank template `1.4.0` sets `enable_auto_consolidation: false`, and `BankTemplate.load` refuses a research template where the field is missing or true; replay and evaluation banks drop the field, so they keep the server's default. A `consolidate` job (`atlas.retention.consolidation`: pausable, provider `codex`, one unit per request Atlas submits) decides under a per-bank advisory lock, asks Hindsight to consolidate, records the operation and polls it, bounded by `ATLAS_CONSOLIDATE_POLL_TIMEOUT_SECONDS`; a run still going stays `submitted` and the next job follows it. Nothing is submitted when no section was retained since the last completed run (`nothing_retained`) or while a retain, poll or reprocess job is queued or running (`retains_pending`); both rules also hold for a request by hand. The worker enqueues one backfill-class job a day from `ATLAS_CONSOLIDATE_AT` (04:30 UTC), retried hourly up to `ATLAS_CONSOLIDATE_MAX_TRIES`. `atlas memory consolidate [--key K] [--backfill]`. The health read gains `consolidation` (last requested, last completed, sections retained since); metric `atlas_consolidations_total{outcome}`. Migration `0069_memory_consolidation` (one `submitted` row per bank), `down_revision` `0068`.
- **Why a table of its own:** `hindsight_operation` has a CHECK on its kind and a NOT NULL `source_version_id`; a consolidation belongs to no Source Version.
- **How a running consolidation is known:** Atlas's own record. Only an unfiltered operations listing was recorded, and with auto-consolidation off the only consolidation running in the bank is one Atlas asked for. Hindsight's `deduplicated` answer is stored on the row.
- **Fake:** the derived template import ignores `bank.enable_auto_consolidation: false` when it compares a manifest with the recording (listed in `docs/decisions.md` with the fake's other derivations).
- **Tests** (WSL, the shared Postgres and Silo; actual results): `tests/integration/test_consolidation.py` 8 passed (red first: all 8 failed before the code existed). With the neighbouring modules, run by the lead on the implementer's uncommitted work: `test_consolidation`, `test_mental_models`, `test_bank_template`, `test_quota_pacing`, `test_queue_pause`, `test_migrations`, `test_replay`, `test_memory_health`, `test_evaluations`, `tests/unit/test_hindsight_contract`, `tests/unit/test_hindsight_fake_derivations`: **198 passed**. `ruff format --check`, `ruff check` and strict `pyright`: clean. The API client regenerated.
- **Not run:** the full suite, the e2e, `scripts/ci.sh`, any live suite. **Fixture-only:** whether the cluster's Hindsight takes `enable_auto_consolidation` from a template import is not verified live; the runbook gives the bank-config call as the fallback. On production the owner set the field to false by hand on 2026-10-02.
- **How this entry came to be written by the lead:** the implementer's session ended before its last steps (the neighbouring tests, this entry, the commit). The lead ran the checks above on its worktree, wrote this entry from its report and its code, and committed.
- **Shared files touched, additively:** `jobs/budget.py`, `jobs/handlers.py`, `settings.py`, `metrics.py`, `cli.py`, the gateway and its models, the template (the field and the version only), `tests/fakes/hindsight.py`; `test_mental_models` reads template `1.4.0`; `CODEX_KINDS` and `PAUSABLE_KINDS` gain `consolidate`; the migration head in `test_migrations` is `0069`.

## 2026-10-02: memory-quality ticket 08, chunk-exact pointers

- **What:** pointer recalls (the Scout's and the Skeptic's) ask for `include.chunks`; each resolved fact's chunk is located verbatim in its section (`atlas.research.chunks`: the answer's chunk when its whole text occurs once in the section, else the document's chunks listed through the existing `HindsightGateway.document_chunks` and searched in `chunk_index` order, ticket 01's rule). A pointer whose fact's chunk lies in the pointed section is `placed_by` `chunk` and stores the chunk's ID and span (never its text); an observation's is placed by its source facts' chunks; otherwise `match`. Passage selection reads the window a chunk-placed pointer's span starts in (tag `pointer:<n>:chunk`, the Skeptic's `pointer:<item>:chunk`), when the parse read has the section at the recorded offsets. The investigation read and a Research Snapshot's pointers carry `placed_by`, `chunk_id`, `chunk_char_start`/`_end`; the card's `read` gives `pointers_placed_by` per document; `POST /api/v1/memory/recall` takes `include_chunks` (each resolved source gets its chunk span; every source its `chunk_id`). Migration `0065` (down `0066`; the lead re-chains). The investigation page shows which rule placed each pointer.
- **Conformance:** check 7's gate (`placed_by` in the OpenAPI) is lifted; the check itself was not changed (it tests the precondition and was right about the shape). The rehearsal fixture turns on `derive_chunks()`; the pinned pending checks are now exactly `[8]`, and check 7 passes with every fact placed (19 of 19 in `scripts/memory-conformance.sh --rehearse`).
- **Fake:** `derive_chunks(size=3000)`, `script_fact_chunk`, `script_chunk_text`, `derived_chunks` (off by default; listed in `docs/decisions.md` with the fake's other derivations): the chunk listing and `include.chunks` of derived documents, built from `chunks/03-list-chunks` and `recall_options/07-include-chunks`. Without it a derived document lists no chunk and every pointer is placed by match, so earlier tests keep their answers.
- **Files:** `backend/atlas/research/chunks.py` (new), `backend/atlas/research/{provenance,service}.py`, `backend/atlas/investigations/{pointers,tasks,skeptic,model,coverage}.py`, `backend/atlas/claims/selection.py`, `backend/atlas/snapshots.py`, `backend/atlas/db/migrations/versions/0065_chunk_placed_pointers.py` (new), `frontend/app/investigation/page.tsx`, `frontend/lib/api/{openapi.json,schema.ts}` (regenerated), `tests/fakes/hindsight.py`, `tests/integration/{test_investigations,test_memory_conformance,test_migrations}.py`, `tests/unit/{test_chunk_location (new),test_hindsight_fake_derivations,test_passage_selection}.py`, `AGENTS.md`, `CONTEXT.md`, `docs/decisions.md`, the ticket.
- **Tests** (WSL, the shared Postgres; actual results):
  - **Red run seen:** `test_investigations.py -k chunk` before the build: the chunk-placement test failed on its first assertion that matters (pointer recalls sent `include: {"source_facts": {}}` without `chunks`). After the build both new integration tests passed (2 passed).
  - New integration tests (`test_investigations.py`): a pointer reads the window its fact's chunk lies in (Lumentum 10-K Item 1, chunk size 1,200: the fact's text, Item 1's first 200 characters, matches the opening window best; its chunk holds the allocation statement; the Investigator's pointer window is the chunk's, tagged `pointer:0:chunk`..., the accepted Claim's `passage_selected_by` and the Evidence tray say so, the card counts `{"chunk": 1}`; the Skeptic's pointers are placed by the chunk and its first passage is the chunk's window); a chunk that is not a slice leaves every pointer `match` on the opening window.
  - Unit tests written after the code (not seen red): `test_chunk_location.py` (the recorded chunks located at 0:603 and 605:813, as the feature matrix found; ordered search; a non-slice moves nothing), the fake's chunk derivation, passage selection with chunk-placed pointers and the parse guard, `placements`.
  - `test_memory_conformance.py`, `test_research.py`, `test_passage_selection.py`, `test_hindsight_fake_derivations.py`, `test_hindsight_contract.py`, `test_hindsight_gateway.py`, `test_migrations.py`: **232 passed** (before the later unit additions); then `test_chunk_location.py`, `test_hindsight_fake_derivations.py`, `test_passage_selection.py`: **75 passed**.

A chunk-placed pointer falls back to match when the parse read lays the section out at other offsets than the recorded one (a re-parse). Every citation source now carries its fact's `chunk_id` (also in reflect citations), an additive field.

## 2026-10-02: memory-quality ticket 09, the entity hop

- **What:** after its recalls, the Scout's task hops from each company the round reads (seeds first, then the companies its recall pointers name; at most `ATLAS_ENTITY_HOP_MAX_COMPANIES`, 6) to the theme's facts that carry the company's entity: the entity found by exact canonical name (`legal_name`) in the entity listing, never by parsing the memory list's comma-joined `entities`; the facts listed by `entity_memories` (theme tag, `any_strict`), resolved by the provenance resolver, the as-of bound applied in Atlas from each fact's Source Version. A fact from another company's document, in a section no recall pointer of the round already points at, becomes an **entity pointer** (`reading_pointer.query_kind` `entity`, `query_company_id`, `entity_id`; migration `0064`, `down_revision` `0066`), newest first, at most `ATLAS_ENTITY_HOP_MAX_FACTS` (20) per company. Entity pointers weigh `ATLAS_ENTITY_HOP_POINTER_WEIGHT` (0.5) / rank in the ranking of pointed companies (the weight recorded in the Scout's artifacts; `pointers`/`best_rank` stay the recall pointers', `best_rank` null for a hop-only company, `entity_pointers` counts the rest), and are passage selection's third channel (`entity_pointer:<company_id>`, in turn after recall pointers, capped at the ceiling under it; unchanged dealing without them). Failed listings are `entity_listing_failed` events; a company without an entity is `no_entity`. The read gives `entity_pointers` and `entity_hops` apart from `pointers`; the card's `not_read` gains `entity_pointers` and `channels`; a document's `selections` counts `entity_pointer`; the snapshot carries `entity_id` and `entity_pointer_selections`. The page shows the hop (`EntityPointers`) and the channels of a company not read. No LLM call, no budget; co-mention makes no edge, no Evidence, and no text reaches a role.
- **Conformance check 8:** its gate (`entity_hop_max_companies`) is lifted and it **passes** in the rehearsal unchanged (it needed only the fake's entity-memory derivation); `test_memory_conformance.py` now pins the pending checks to `[7]`.
- **Files:** `backend/atlas/investigations/entity_hop.py` (new), `backend/atlas/db/migrations/versions/0064_entity_pointers.py` (new), `backend/atlas/investigations/{companies,pointers,tasks,service,model,coverage}.py`, `backend/atlas/claims/selection.py`, `backend/atlas/settings.py`, `backend/atlas/snapshots.py`, `backend/atlas/research/probes.py` (`best_rank` type), `backend/atlas/evaluation/stubs.py` (an empty entity listing), `tests/fakes/hindsight.py` (derivation: memory list by entity of derived facts, `fail_entity_memories`), `tests/integration/{test_investigations,test_migrations,test_memory_conformance}.py`, `tests/unit/{test_passage_selection,test_pointed_companies}.py`, `frontend/{app/investigation/page.tsx,lib/workbench.ts,lib/api/*,unit/workbench.test.ts}`, `AGENTS.md`, `CONTEXT.md` ("Entity pointer"), `docs/decisions.md` ("The entity hop", and the derivation in the fake's list), the ticket.

## 2026-10-02: pilot-fixes ticket 26, the Investigator's answers fit the schema

- **The cause** (read from the saved fifth run, `role-calls.json`; no production text copied into a test): 32 role calls had a failed first attempt, 29 of them the Investigator's. Of those 29, 21 failed only for fields the schema doesn't have (`subject_name` in 20 answers, `claim_id` in 10, once each `no_claims_reason`, `predicate_name`, `questions`), 5 left out `object_text`, and 3 left out `object_company_id` and `object_name` on a product Claim. The strict schema does forbid extra properties (`additionalProperties: false` on every object, every property required: `atlas.roles.contract._check_strict`) and is sent as `response_format`, but MiniMax through LiteLLM does not enforce it: 25 of the 72 first answers came in a code fence, and keys come in the model's own order (`predicate` first in 33). So the model writes the shape from the prompt, and `investigator.v8` never lists a Claim's fields: it describes them in prose across its rules. The answer pairs `object_company_id` with `object_name`, and the model makes the subject symmetric (`subject_name`, null or the name from `request.companies`); `claim_id` (`"c1"`, `"1"`) numbers the Claims the way the passages are numbered (`p1`, …); nothing said a Claim has no ID. Neither field is anywhere in the request.
- **Built:** (1) the role caller drops a field the response model doesn't name (pydantic `extra_forbidden`, any depth), validates the rest again and records the paths on the attempt (`llm_call.ignored_fields`, migration `0072`, `down_revision` `0068`); no repair for it, for every role; a missing or malformed field is repaired as before, and only the remaining errors go into the repair. (2) `ProposedClaim` reads a left-out object field as null when another of the three object fields is given or the predicate is `capacity_constrained` (a `mode="before"` validator, so the JSON schema is unchanged); with none given, another predicate still fails and is repaired. (3) `investigator.v9`: "The answer" lists the twelve fields in the schema's order, all present, no other, and names `subject_name` and `claim_id` as fields that don't exist; ticket 22's rule as one bullet beside the `quote` rule ("an analyst's question is context, not a statement by the company"; ticket 22's log entry was not yet written when this was, so the wording is this ticket's). (4) Repairs per role: `repairs` on `GET /api/v1/runs/{id}/role-calls` and on the investigation's `usage` (`atlas.roles.run_repairs`); the investigation page shows them under the budget use. Rules: `docs/decisions.md`, "Roles: an unknown field is ignored, a missing one repaired".
- **Files:** `backend/atlas/roles/caller.py` (`_validate`, `_without`, the attempt's `ignored_fields`), `records.py` (`ignored_fields`, `repairs`, `run_repairs`), `__init__.py`, `investigator.py` (v9, the object-field validator), `prompts/investigator.v9.md` (new; v8 untouched); `backend/atlas/investigations/model.py` (`Usage.repairs`); `backend/atlas/db/migrations/versions/0072_llm_call_ignored_fields.py` (new); `frontend/app/investigation/page.tsx`, `frontend/unit/workbench.test.ts` (fixture), `frontend/lib/api/` regenerated; tests below; `AGENTS.md`, `docs/decisions.md`.
- **Tests** (WSL, `.scratch/tools/wsl_checks.sh`):
  - New: `test_an_unknown_extra_field_is_ignored_and_recorded_without_a_repair` and `test_a_missing_required_field_is_still_repaired_and_extra_fields_are_not_named_in_it` (`test_role_calls.py`; the first also checks the strict schema sent is unchanged); `test_an_answer_with_extra_fields_and_unneeded_object_fields_left_out_needs_no_repair` and `test_a_claim_with_no_object_field_and_a_predicate_that_needs_one_is_still_repaired` (`test_claims.py`, a synthetic Lumentum document); `test_the_investigator_s_strict_schema_still_requires_every_field_and_forbids_others` and `test_investigator_v9_lists_the_claim_s_fields_and_names_the_ones_it_has_not` (`tests/unit/test_roles.py`); `test_chat_completions_recorded_before_0072_ignored_no_field` (`test_migrations.py`, head `0072`). Changed: the quarantine test (its extra `confidence` is now ignored, only `answer` is an error), the role-call usage tests (`repairs`, `ignored_fields`), `test_bottleneck_prompts.py` (v9 and its phrases), the investigation's `usage` (`repairs: {}`; `{"scout": 3}` in the keeps-failing test), `investigator.v8` → `v9` in `test_claims.py`, `test_counterparties.py`, `test_relationships.py`.
  - **Red seen:** the five role-call tests (new and changed: no `ignored_fields`, no `repairs`, the extra field asked to be repaired) and the v9 unit test (version 8), before the code. The two new `test_claims.py` tests and the migration test were written before the code but first run after it: no red run of them was seen.
  - **Results:** `tests/integration/test_role_calls.py` + `test_claims.py`: 50 passed. `tests/unit` + `test_migrations.py` + `test_evaluations.py` (the scripted evaluation cases) + `test_counterparties.py` + `test_relationships.py`: 934 passed, 1 failed (`test_bottleneck_prompts.py`, still expecting v8); fixed, then `test_bottleneck_prompts.py` + `test_roles.py`: 17 passed. `test_investigations.py -k` the three tests touched: 3 passed. `ruff format --check`, `ruff check`, `pyright`: clean. Frontend `lint`, `typecheck`, `test` (42 passed).
  - **Not run:** the rest of `test_investigations.py`, the full suite, the e2e, `scripts/gen_api_client.sh --check` (the client regenerated with the script's two commands), any live run.
- **Fixture-only vs live:** nothing live. Whether `investigator.v9` stops MiniMax adding the fields is for the next pilot run; the caller no longer repairs them either way.
- **Deviations:** the ticket names a missing `object_text`; the same rule also covers a left-out `object_company_id`/`object_name` (3 of the 29 calls), read the same way. Repairs are counted per role on the run's role calls as well as on the investigation's usage. The Skeptic's one repair (an extra `source_version_id` with a missing `kind`) and the Financial Analyst's (missing `assertion_id`/`observation_id`) would still be repaired for the missing fields; the first answer of the Analyst's quarantined call, whose only error was an extra `data_gaps`, would now be accepted.

## 2026-10-02: memory-quality ticket 12: the backfill to the current profile

- **Live, first (4 LLM requests of the cap of 8):** on the local spike Hindsight 0.10.2 (`spikes/hindsight/delete_and_retain.py`, MiniMax-M3 through LiteLLM, thinking disabled) one synthetic document (fictional companies) was retained (1 request, 2 world facts), consolidated (1 request, 2 observations), its document deleted (`DELETE .../documents/{id}`: 200, `memory_units_deleted: 2`; afterwards the document 404, no fact, no observation, no entity), and retained again under the same ID with a new context, one more tag and given entities (1 request: 2 new facts with **new IDs**, the new context and tags, the given entities among theirs) and consolidated again (1 request). Every request and response is under `spikes/hindsight/recordings/delete_and_retain/` (25 files), the counts in `spikes/hindsight/results/delete-and-retain.json` and `delete-and-retain-llm-requests.json`; a row in `docs/hindsight-feature-matrix.md`. The recordings were scanned for the LiteLLM URL and key (none). The two spike containers were stopped, the bank deleted, port 8888 only. Not observed: an observation built from facts of the deleted document and of another document. `GET .../observations` is 405 (the observations are the memory list's `fact_type: observation` rows; recordings 06, 11, 17, 23 are that 405, so the `observations` lists in the results file are empty by that).
- **Built to what was observed:** `atlas memory backfill --company <slug>... | --theme <slug> [--max-sections N] [--key K]` enqueues a backfill-class `memory_backfill` job per company. Its first step takes the company's sections `failed` or `cancelled`, then `completed` or `zero_fact` under an older `retain_profile`, failed and cancelled first, then call transcripts, then the rest, newest documents first, at most `max_sections` (counted by `memory_replacement` rows of the run, so a retry after a pause does not take more); deletes each Hindsight document (a 404 is no error) and in one transaction per Source Version resets the sections to `pending` (audited `memory_document.backfill_reset`), records `memory_replacement` and enqueues a backfill-class `retain`, which submits through the existing retain path. It then follows itself (`step` 1, 2, ...) behind the retain and poll jobs, at most 20 waits, and asks for a `consolidate` (backfill class); a run that takes nothing enqueues nothing. `--theme` orders companies the pilot's seeds first, thinnest in Memory first; `--max-sections` is allotted along that order.
- **Replaced memories:** migration `0067` `memory_replacement` (insert-only; the memories a deleted document held). The provenance resolver reads a memory Hindsight answers 404 for, found there, as `unverified` with the reason `memory_replaced` (new `UnresolvedReason`), not `broken`. `GET /api/v1/memory/health` sections gain `at_profile` and `below_profile`. The API client was regenerated.
- **Files:** `backend/atlas/retention/backfill.py` (new), `backend/atlas/retention/handlers.py`, `health.py`, `backend/atlas/research/provenance.py`, `backend/atlas/hindsight/{gateway,models,__init__}.py` (`delete_document`, `DocumentDeleted`), `backend/atlas/cli.py`, `backend/atlas/db/migrations/versions/0067_memory_replacement.py`, `tests/fakes/hindsight.py` (document deletion, derived from the recording; fact IDs change after a delete), `tests/integration/test_memory_backfill.py` (new, 6 tests), `tests/unit/test_hindsight_contract.py` (the 25 recordings classified, 4 tests), `tests/integration/test_memory_health.py`, `test_migrations.py` (head `0067`), `test_queue_pause.py` (`memory_backfill` pausable), `frontend/lib/api/`, `docs/{runbooks,decisions,hindsight-feature-matrix}.md`, `AGENTS.md`, the spike script and recordings.
- **Tests (WSL, the local Postgres and Silo; actual results):** `tests/integration/test_memory_backfill.py`: **6 passed** (before the final format and the health-test fix: `test_memory_health`, `test_migrations`, `test_queue_pause`, `test_research`, `test_retention`, `test_consolidation` and the new module: 89 passed, 4 failed in `test_memory_health` on the two new section-count fields, since fixed). After the fix: `test_memory_health` + `tests/unit/test_hindsight_contract.py`: **109 passed**; `tests/unit`: 933 passed, 1 failed (`test_every_recording_is_classified`, since fixed with the new recordings). Red seen: with the `memory_backfill` handler unregistered, 4 of the 6 integration tests failed (no handler for the job kind: nothing deleted, nothing retained again); the two that passed need no handler (the command's refusals; the theme order, which is the CLI's plan). The tests were written after the first implementation pass in the same sitting, not before it; the red run was made afterwards by disabling the handler.
- **Static:** `ruff format --check`, `ruff check`, `pyright`: clean (see the last run in the report). Frontend gates not run (only the generated client changed).
- **Live vs fixture:** the delete-then-retain path is live-recorded (above); the backfill itself runs against the recorded fake only. Nothing was run against the cluster Hindsight or production.
- **Deviations:** `memory_replaced` is a reason under the state `unverified`, not a fourth state (a new state would ripple through the API schema and the frontend; the ticket said "the resolver's states gain it if the fake shows a replaced memory disappears", and the recording shows it does). The pointer acceptance box is not ticked (see the ticket's note). The estimate of sections a night in the runbook is arithmetic from the budgets, not measured at scale.
- **Next:** run it on production for the pilot's seeds (Lumentum and Coherent are the thickest; MACOM, Marvell and Ciena the thinnest), watch `GET /api/v1/memory/health`, then decide the night's `--max-sections` from the first window's operations.

## 2026-10-02: pilot-fix ticket 29: every as-of reader uses the corrected availability

- **Classification of every SQL text in `backend/atlas` (outside migrations) that compares a Source Version's availability with a bound parameter, as-of, cutoff or since:**
  - Raw column, must use the view (fixed): `investigations/tasks.py` pointed documents, latest documents, document floor (`_document_floor`); `investigations/skeptic.py` `_catalog` and the fallback documents (`available_at <= :as_of`).
  - Raw column in Python after a raw read, must use the view (fixed): `scenarios/sources.py` `_assertion` (the Assertion's Source Version `available_at`, compared with `as_of` in `_assertion_problem`).
  - Already on the view: `replay/service.py` (the cutoff selection and the leakage count), `retention/service.py` (`since`), `retention/triage.py` (predecessor choice), `research/provenance.py` (so the pointers' and entity hop's `source.available_at > as_of`), `financials/service.py`, `snapshots.py`, `ledger/reads.py`.

## 2026-10-02: memory-quality ticket 13: a layer's question is also asked of that layer's facts

- **Built:** a Scout query may carry a supply-chain layer, and one that does is recalled a second time with the theme's scope AND the layer's label tag (`tag_groups`), beside its theme recall. The Scout's queries had no layer, so `scout.v5` (new prompt file; `ScoutQuery.layer`, required and nullable like `filing_phrase`) asks for one; Atlas keeps it only if it names a layer of `atlas.claims.LAYER_NAMES` (`discovery_query.layer`, shown by `GET /api/v1/discoveries/{id}`). Migration `0073` (down_revision `0067`, to be re-chained): `discovery_query.layer`, and `reading_pointer.scope` (`theme` | `theme_layer`) with `layer`, the unique key gaining `scope`; the downgrade refuses while a layer pointer exists. The gateway's `recall` takes a `TagGroups` (a compound filter, groups AND-ed) beside `TagScope`; `ResearchScope.layer` makes the public recall ask the same (422 for an unknown layer; a reflect refuses a layer, `layer_scope_unsupported`). `record_pointers` takes an optional `layer_recall`; both recalls' pointers are stored with their scope, a memory both return makes one pointer per recall (the weight rule unchanged and stated in the decision entry), an empty layer recall is counted (`layer_recalls_empty`, event `layer_recalls_empty_for`) and is no failure, a failed one is a `pointer_recall_failed` event naming the layer. The Scout task's artifacts gain `layer_recalls`, `layer_recalls_failed`, `layer_recalls_empty`, `layer_memories_recalled`, `layer_pointers`. The investigation page shows a layer pointer's scope (`pointerScope`).
- **Files:** `backend/atlas/db/migrations/versions/0073_layer_aware_pointers.py` (new), `backend/atlas/roles/scout.py`, `backend/atlas/roles/prompts/scout.v5.md` (new), `backend/atlas/discovery/service.py`, `backend/atlas/hindsight/{models,gateway,__init__}.py`, `backend/atlas/research/service.py`, `backend/atlas/investigations/{pointers,tasks,model}.py`, `frontend/lib/workbench.ts`, `frontend/app/investigation/page.tsx`, `frontend/unit/workbench.test.ts`, `frontend/lib/api/{openapi.json,schema.ts}` (regenerated), `tests/fakes/hindsight.py` (derived `tag_groups`), `tests/integration/{test_investigations,test_research,test_discovery,test_migrations}.py`, `tests/unit/{test_hindsight_fake_derivations,test_bottleneck_prompts}.py`, `docs/decisions.md` ("Layer-aware pointer recall"; the fake's derivation listed beside its others), `AGENTS.md`, the ticket.

## 2026-10-02: a switch that stops every consolidation (0.4.1; memory-quality ticket 19 follow-up)

- **Built:** `ATLAS_CONSOLIDATION_ENABLED` (default true). Off, `consolidation_schedules` returns none (no daily run) and every `consolidate` job, daily, by hand or the memory backfill's, is decided `skipped` with the reason `consolidation_off` before any request (migration `0074`: the skip-reason check gains the value; `atlas_consolidations_total{outcome="skipped_consolidation_off"}`). Released as `v0.4.1`.

## 2026-10-03: a queued retain no longer holds the consolidation (0.4.2)

- **Built:** the consolidation's `retains_pending` skip now holds only while a retain may be in Hindsight: a `retain` or `reprocess` job running, or a `poll_operation` job queued or running. A queued retain has submitted nothing, so the backfill's retains held behind the `hindsight_minimax` budget no longer keep the bank unconsolidated until the whole backfill is done (`docs/decisions.md`, "Atlas decides when Memory consolidates", the 0.4.2 note). The memory backfill's own wait (a company is done when no retain job is left) is unchanged; it keeps its own list of kinds.
- **Files:** `backend/atlas/retention/consolidation.py`, `backend/atlas/retention/backfill.py`, `tests/integration/test_consolidation.py`, `docs/decisions.md`, `AGENTS.md`. With it, ticket 10's gold supersessions: `tests/integration/test_evaluations.py` counts the active cases only and names EV-CON-002, EV-RST-002 and EV-SYN-002.
- **Tests (WSL; actual results):** new `test_a_retain_waiting_its_turn_does_not_hold_a_consolidation_one_in_flight_does` (a backfill-class retain queued outside a closed window: the consolidation completes; a `poll_operation` claimed by another worker: skipped `retains_pending`). Red against the old rule (`assert 'skipped' == 'completed'`), then green. `test_consolidation` + `test_memory_backfill`: 16 passed. `test_evaluations` + the gold and scoring unit tests: 16 passed (all 12 active cases pass in fake mode). Static clean.

## 2026-10-03: memory-quality ticket 20: a consolidation followed to its end and counted by rounds (for 0.4.3)

- **Built:** the `consolidate` job follows a run's whole chain of rounds (the operation it requested and every consolidation operation of the bank created after it, read from `GET …/operations?type=consolidation`, a page of 100 until the requested one), records each round once (`memory_consolidation_round`) and counts it against a new provider, `hindsight_consolidation` (unit `rounds`, `ATLAS_CONSOLIDATE_BUDGET_ROUNDS`, default 40, the usual window and backfill limit; `consolidate` left `codex`, whose sweep no longer counts consolidation requests). A run is `completed` only when no round is pending or processing and the last ended `completed`, recording the bank's `pending_consolidation` (`GET …/stats`). When the window's rounds reach the job class's limit while a round is running, the job cancels that round (`DELETE …/operations/{id}`; 409 ignored) and records the run `stopped_at_budget` (a retry outcome of the daily schedule; the next try waits for the window, then asks again). Before submitting, a consolidation of the bank pending or processing that Atlas did not request makes the job skip with the new reason `other_consolidation_running` (also a retry outcome): never counted, never cancelled. Gateway: `consolidation_operations`, `cancel_operation`, `bank_stats` (models `ListedOperation`, `OperationPage`, `OperationCancelled`, `BankStats`). Migration `0075` (down `0074`): the round table, `memory_consolidation.rounds`/`last_operation_id`/`pending_consolidation`, the new status and skip reason, the provider. Health read: each run shows `rounds`, `last_operation_id`, `pending_consolidation`; metrics: `atlas_consolidations_total{outcome}` gains `stopped_at_budget` and `skipped_other_consolidation_running`, and `atlas_consolidation_rounds_total`.
- **Files:** `backend/atlas/retention/consolidation.py`, `backend/atlas/jobs/budget.py`, `backend/atlas/hindsight/{gateway,models,__init__}.py`, `backend/atlas/metrics.py`, `backend/atlas/settings.py`, `backend/atlas/db/migrations/versions/0075_consolidation_rounds.py` (new), `tests/fakes/hindsight.py` (the rounds derivation), `tests/integration/{test_consolidation,test_quota_pacing,test_migrations}.py`, `tests/unit/test_hindsight_fake_derivations.py`, `frontend/lib/api/{openapi.json,schema.ts}` (regenerated), `.env.example`, `docs/decisions.md` (the 0.4.3 note; the fake's new derivation listed), `docs/runbooks.md` ("Consolidation"), `AGENTS.md`, the ticket.
- **Tests (WSL; actual results):** new, at the worker seam: `test_a_run_of_three_rounds_is_followed_across_jobs_and_counted_by_rounds`, `test_a_run_that_reaches_the_rounds_budget_is_cancelled_and_the_next_try_completes_it`, `test_a_consolidation_atlas_did_not_request_is_neither_counted_nor_cancelled`, `test_a_spent_codex_window_no_longer_holds_the_job` (replaces ticket 19's codex-hold test); the fake: `test_derived_consolidation_rounds_chain_list_cancel_and_count`. Updated: ticket 19's first job test (one round in the new budget, none in codex; the new record fields), the metric expectations, `test_quota_pacing` (codex kinds without `consolidate`, the provider lists), the migration head. **Red:** the tests were written after the fake, the migration and the budget change but before the following logic in `consolidation.py`; that red run failed the three new run tests (`KeyError: 'rounds'`, `'completed' == 'running'`, `KeyError: 'reason'`) and ticket 19's updated test (`KeyError: 'rounds'`); the codex-window test already passed then (the budget change was in). Green after: `test_consolidation` + `test_migrations` 27 passed; then `test_consolidation` + `test_memory_backfill` + `test_queue_pause` + `test_quota_pacing` + `test_migrations` + `test_hindsight_fake_derivations` 82 passed; `test_replay` + `test_hindsight_contract` + `test_settings` (with some of the above) 209 passed; `test_memory_conformance` + `test_observation_scopes` + `test_mental_models` 24 passed. `ruff format --check`, `ruff check`, strict `pyright`: clean. API client regenerated. Full suite and e2e not run (the runners do).
- **Live vs fixture:** fixtures only. The rounds, the operations listing (with `type`/`status` filters), the cancel and `GET …/stats` are derivations of the Hindsight fake built to the shapes the lead read live on 2026-10-03 (the ticket), none recorded: the listing entries are `operations/01-list`'s recorded consolidation entry with named fields changed; the cancel, its 409 and the stats bodies are hand-written to the documented `CancelOperationResponse` and `BankStatsResponse`. Not seen live: how `created_at` orders a chained round after the one before it, and that a cancelled round is never followed.
- **Deviations:** (1) A new skip reason, `other_consolidation_running`, though the ticket lists the skip rules as unchanged: the listing has no link from a round to the one before it, so Atlas tells its chain from another only by asking while nothing else is consolidating; the three existing rules are as they were. (2) Budget semantics read literally: a round is cancelled as soon as the rounds counted reach the limit, so with a budget of N a run gets N−1 whole rounds and a cut one per window (the cut one is counted). (3) Rounds that run between job attempts (about 8 an hour while the job waits for its next try) are counted when seen and can overrun a window; the stop then cancels the round running at that moment (fixed after the lead's audit: the entry below, follow-ups every 300 s). (4) A run recorded before `0075` keeps `rounds` 0 and its `codex` unit.
- **Next:** release 0.4.3 with consolidation still off; then the lead's live check of the listing order and the cancel on production before consolidation is turned on.

## 2026-10-03: memory-quality ticket 20, the lead's audit: a run still going is followed every few minutes

- **Defect (the lead's audit of `3df27b4`):** a run still going was looked at again only by the hourly retry, within the day's tries, so up to ~8 rounds ran unwatched between looks and, once the tries were used, the chain ran unwatched until the next day.
- **Built:** `ConsolidationFollow`, a worker schedule (beside the daily one; active whenever Hindsight and consolidation are on, even with `ATLAS_CONSOLIDATE_AT` empty): while a run of the bank is `submitted` and no consolidate job is queued or running, it enqueues a follow-up `consolidate` job `ATLAS_CONSOLIDATE_FOLLOW_SECONDS` (new setting, 300) after the last consolidate job's `looked_at` (a new artifact: when the job finished looking), keyed `consolidate:<bank>:follow:<run>:<n>`, of the class of the job that requested the run. Payload `follow` (the run): the job only follows (never decides or submits); a run already ended gives `follow_ended`. Not a daily try, not bound to the day. **The queue has no delayed enqueue** (no not-before on a job); rather than add one, the delay uses the worker's schedules, which the daily tries already use for their hourly retry: no queue change.
- **Files:** `backend/atlas/retention/consolidation.py`, `backend/atlas/settings.py`, `tests/integration/test_consolidation.py`, `.env.example`, `docs/decisions.md` (the 0.4.3 note: the follow-ups, the overshoot bound, N−1 whole rounds plus the one cut short), `docs/runbooks.md`, `AGENTS.md`. Merged `origin/main` (4915fab, the corrected conformance checks) first; no conflict.
- **Tests (WSL; actual results):** new `test_a_run_still_going_is_followed_every_interval_past_the_daily_tries_until_it_ends` (daily try 1 of 1 requests a four-round run; nothing at 299 s; a follow-up at 301 s and after each round, backfill class, keyed by run and counter; completed within the hour; no new request; nothing after the run ends) and `test_a_chain_longer_than_the_budget_is_cancelled_within_one_follow_interval` (budget 3; two rounds end unwatched; the follow-up 301 s later counts them and cancels the third). Red first, with the follow schedule switched off: both failed (`assert 0 == 1`, no job enqueued); green after restoring it. `test_consolidation`: 15 passed. `test_memory_conformance` + `test_quota_pacing` + `test_queue_pause` + `test_migrations` + `test_memory_backfill` + `test_mental_models` + `test_settings`: 102 passed. Static clean.
- **Live vs fixture:** fixtures only, as above.
- **Next:** release 0.4.3.

## 2026-10-03: shorter releases: the suite runs once per commit

- **Built:** `release.yml` gains a `verified` job (GitHub's API: has `ci.yml` passed on the tagged commit, on any branch?); the `ci` job runs only when it has not, and `publish` needs the commit to have passed CI there or here (fails closed: a failed `verified` publishes nothing). `ci.yml` skips pushes that change only `.scratch/**` (except `.scratch/pilot/pilot-plan.md`, which `tests/unit/test_memory_probes.py` reads) or `docs/implementation-log.md`. `docs/deployment.md` describes both.
- **Why:** a release ran the 30-minute suite twice for one commit (the integration branch, then the tag: 0.4.2's took 32 minutes before a 1.5-minute publish), and every tracker comment pushed to main started a full run on the owner's runners.
- **Tests:** none local (workflow files; YAML parsed). The first check is the next release: its `verified` job must find the integration branch's green run and skip `ci`.
- **Next:** parallel tests (pytest-xdist), on a branch.
## 2026-10-03: the test suite in parallel (pytest-xdist)

- **Built:** CI runs the tests with `uv run pytest -n auto --dist load` (`scripts/ci.sh`); plain `uv run pytest` still runs serially (no `-n` in `addopts`). `--dist load` (tests dealt out one at a time) rather than `loadfile`/`loadscope`: every test already has its own database, bucket, archive directory (`tmp_path`, per worker) and ephemeral localhost ports (`tests/fakes/serve.py` binds port 0), and grouping by file would leave `test_investigations.py` (~4,900 lines) as the tail. The two module-scoped fixtures (`test_archive_object_lock`'s locked bucket, `test_archive_provisioning`'s provisioned bucket/user/policy) have random names, so a worker that gets tests of such a module provisions its own.
- **Isolation fixed:** (1) `test_every_gold_case_passes_in_fake_mode_and_the_run_is_stored_and_served` counted every `atlas_eval_%` database on the server; with the module's other evaluation tests on other workers (or another run on the same server) it would see their case databases. It now checks the run's own databases are gone (`atlas_eval_<run id[:12]>_`, the runner's naming; the run ID from the CLI's summary): the same assertion, scoped to what this run created. Nothing else broke under parallelism: the audit test's cluster-wide `atlas_app` role already tolerated a concurrent `CREATE ROLE`; the readiness test only reads the shared `atlas` database; no test mutates `os.environ` (only `monkeypatch`, per process) or writes to a fixed path.
- **Faster setup:** `database_url` no longer migrates each test's database: a session-scoped `migrated_template` (one per xdist worker, `atlas_test_template_<worker>_<random>`, dropped at the end) is migrated once and each test's database is `CREATE DATABASE … TEMPLATE` of it (alembic's `NullPool` leaves no connection on the template). A fresh create + migrate took ~3 s per test under WSL; a copy takes well under 0.2 s. Migrating from empty is still tested (`empty_database_url`, `test_migrations`, `test_audit`'s role test). The migrations seed no time-dependent rows (only `queue_pause` id 1), so a copy is the same database.
- **Files:** `pyproject.toml`, `uv.lock` (`pytest-xdist` dev dependency), `scripts/ci.sh`, `tests/integration/conftest.py`, `tests/integration/test_evaluations.py`.
- **Tests (WSL, `-n 8`, 24 cores; actual results):** Silo (127.0.0.1:59000) was not running on this machine (connection refused; not started, per the brief), so the 35 S3 tests (`test_archive_contract[s3]`, `test_archive_object_lock`, `test_archive_provisioning`, three in `test_readiness`) errored and one failed in every run, the same in serial. Run 1 (before the template, `--dist load`): 1607 passed, 1 failed + 35 errors, all S3; wall 1057 s. Run 2 (final code): 1607 passed, the same 36 S3 only; wall 776 s. Run 3 (final code) was killed at 21% by the session's low-memory reaper (Windows host), not by a test; up to there its results matched run 2's (the same S3 errors, nothing else). A second full run of the final code is the runners' to do. Serial time not measured locally (the runners' serial baseline: 1,643 tests, 1807 s). Static (`ruff format --check`, `ruff check`, strict `pyright`): clean.
- **Live vs fixture:** no live service involved.
- **Deviations / open:** the S3 tests' parallel behaviour was not exercised here (Silo down); their names are random per test or module, so no clash is expected. `-n auto` on a runner pod with no CPU limit starts one worker per node CPU; Postgres's default `max_connections` (100) has room for roughly a dozen or more workers at a few connections each, not measured; if the runner shows "too many clients", cap it with `--maxprocesses`.
- **Next:** the lead runs the branch on the runners and reads the test phase's time.

## 2026-10-03: the suite in parallel, on the runners

- CI run 37145568748 (`integrate/parallel-tests`, `pytest -n auto --maxprocesses 8 --dist load`): 8 workers, **1,643 passed in 316 s** (5 min 16 s; the serial suite took 1,807 s on 0.4.3's run), the S3 tests included; the whole CI job 7 min 45 s. The lead capped the workers at 8 (the pod has no CPU limit; Postgres allows 100 connections). With the release gate, a release is now one CI run (~8 min) and the publish (~1.5 min).

## 2026-10-03: memory-quality ticket 22, Atlas's records reconciled with Memory every night

- **What:** a read-only `reconcile_memory` job (`atlas.retention.reconciliation`; no LLM, only GETs to Hindsight; not pausable, no budget, interactive class) compares Atlas's records of the research bank with Hindsight and records one insert-only `memory_reconciliation` row per run (migration `0076`, down `0075`): six drift kinds (`section_missing`, `document_unrecorded`, `profile_mismatch`, `observation_scope_outside`, `config_drift`, `consolidation_untracked`), each a count, up to 20 samples and a breakdown, plus the `usage` table (the day's `GET …/llm-requests` by operation beside the `codex`/`hindsight_consolidation`/`hindsight_minimax` units in `provider_usage`; never drift); `status` `clean`, `drift` or `failed` (a listing or read failed: no counts, no usage). Ticket 21's rule is reused: `STUCK` now composes a factored-out `IN_FLIGHT` fragment (same SQL), which the failed/cancelled case uses too. `config_drift` compares every key of the template's `manifest.bank` (so ticket 23's pinned settings are covered), normalised per the lead's message: absent, null, `{}` and `[]` equal at any depth, key order ignored. The worker enqueues one a day from `ATLAS_RECONCILE_AT` (new setting, 03:30 UTC; empty: never), consolidation on or off; `atlas memory reconcile [--key K] [--wait] [--timeout] [--poll-seconds]` (exit 0 only when clean). Visible: health `reconciliation`, `GET /api/v1/memory/reconciliations[/{id}]`, gauges `atlas_memory_drift{kind}` and `atlas_memory_reconciliation_last_success_timestamp_seconds`, counter `atlas_memory_reconciliations_total{status}`, alerts `AtlasMemoryDrift` (for 24h) and `AtlasMemoryReconciliationStale` (36 h).
- **Files:** `backend/atlas/retention/reconciliation.py` (new), `backend/atlas/db/migrations/versions/0076_memory_reconciliation.py` (new), `backend/atlas/hindsight/{models,gateway,__init__}.py` (`documents`, `llm_requests`; `ListedDocument`, `RetainParams`, `DocumentPage`, `LlmRequest`, `LlmRequestPage`: the call's `input`/`output` deliberately unmodelled), `backend/atlas/retention/{backfill,health}.py`, `backend/atlas/api/memory.py`, `backend/atlas/jobs/handlers.py`, `backend/atlas/metrics.py`, `backend/atlas/cli.py`, `backend/atlas/settings.py`, `.env.example`, `configs/prometheus/atlas-alerts.yaml`, `tests/fakes/hindsight.py`, `tests/integration/test_memory_reconciliation.py` (new), `tests/integration/test_migrations.py`, `tests/unit/test_alert_rules.py`, `tests/integration/test_consolidation.py` and `test_mental_models.py` (`reconcile_at=""`, keeping the new schedule out of their scheduled passes), `frontend/lib/api/{openapi.json,schema.ts}`, `docs/decisions.md`, `docs/runbooks.md`, `AGENTS.md`.
- **Tests (WSL, actual results):** red first, honestly ordered: the core was written before the tests, so red was shown by running the new tests against main's backend (production files checked out from `origin/main`, new modules removed): 12 failed. Green on the implementation: 12 passed. The lead's normalisation message came after: `test_a_setting_stored_with_empty_fields_or_reordered_keys_is_not_drift` failed on the unnormalised comparison (`config_drift: ['entity_labels']`), then passed. Affected modules (`test_memory_reconciliation`, `test_consolidation`, `test_mental_models`, `test_memory_health`, `test_memory_backfill`, `test_queue_pause`, `test_migrations`, `test_research`, `test_alert_rules`, `test_hindsight_contract`, `test_cli`): 222 passed; after renaming the usage models (`Usage` clashed with the investigations' OpenAPI schema), reconciliation, health and queue-pause: 32 passed. Ruff and pyright clean; frontend lint, typecheck and unit tests (48) pass.
- **Live vs fixture:** fixture only. The documents listing, the config after a derived import with live changes and the traced LLM calls are new derivations of the Hindsight fake, built to the shapes the lead read live on 2026-10-03 (listed in `docs/decisions.md` with the fake's others); nothing live was called.
- **Deviations / open:** (1) the `llm-requests` listing carries each call's prompt and answer, so a busy day's read is heavy (up to 200 pages of 500); `llm-requests/stats?operation=` would be lighter if it proves too much on production. (2) `profile_mismatch` takes the expected scopes from the document's own `theme:` tags in the listing, not from recomputing the item. (3) A failed run makes the job succeed (artifacts say `failed`), so `AtlasJobsFailing` stays quiet; the stale alert covers it, and it is silent before the first complete run. (4) Alert expressions use metrics exposed even with no run (0s), as `test_queue_pause` requires.
- **Next:** after deploy, `atlas memory reconcile --wait` on production; expect `document_unrecorded` for the 58 stuck sections until the ticket-21 backfill runs, and `observation_scope_outside` for the old scopes.

## 2026-10-06: pilot-review ticket 23, Memory holds the intake window

- **Built:** `atlas memory retire --before <date> [--company]... [--max-sections N] [--key]` and the `memory_retire` job (`atlas.retention.retire`; backfill class, registered pausable like the backfill, no LLM, no budget provider): per company, deletes the Hindsight document of each section of a Source Version available before the date (newest first; any state but `linked`; a `pending` one with a retain in flight waits), sets it `retired`, records it in the insert-only `memory_retirement` (migration `0077`, which also adds `retired` to `memory_document.retain_state`), enqueues a `consolidate`. `--max-sections 0` counts only. Job artifacts: `sections_retired`, `retired_by_state`, `documents_absent`, `memory_units_deleted`, `sections_left`, `pending_consolidation_before`/`_after`, `consolidate_job`. Provenance reads a retired memory `unverified` / `memory_retired`; health counts `retired` (bank, company, memory read); `retire` is a final section state in metrics. Retain's twin link, `retry-failed`, the backfill and its stuck rule, triage and on-demand retain skip `retired`; the reconciliation skips it for `section_missing` and reports a still-listed document as `document_unrecorded`; `sections_retained_since` counts `retired` too, so the consolidation after a retirement is not skipped as `nothing_retained`.
- **Files:** `backend/atlas/retention/retire.py` (new), `backend/atlas/db/migrations/versions/0077_memory_retirement.py` (new), `retention/{handlers,health,reads,reconciliation,service,consolidation}.py`, `research/provenance.py`, `metrics.py`, `cli.py`, `tests/integration/test_memory_retire.py` (new), `test_migrations.py` (head `0077`), `test_queue_pause.py` (`memory_retire` in `PAUSABLE_KINDS`), `frontend/lib/api/` (regenerated), `docs/{decisions,runbooks,implementation-log}.md`, `AGENTS.md`.
- **Live vs fixture:** fixture only. The fake's DELETE answer already carries `memory_units_deleted` (recorded on 0.10.2, `delete_and_retain/09-delete-document`), so no shape was built from the docs alone. Nothing here was run against production or any live Hindsight; whether the invalidated observations rebuild as documented is the Ops step's to read (`pending_consolidation` after the first 50).
- **Deviations / open:** a `linked` section is not changed (it has no document); it keeps pointing at a version whose sections are retired. The ticket's "Not yet" item (scheduled retirement) is for the lead to add to the map's "Not yet specified".

## Frontend ticket 03: the Memory page

- **Files:** `frontend/app/memory/page.tsx`, `frontend/lib/memory.ts` (derivations: rows, depth to scale, facts per version, open items, figures), `frontend/components/version-bar.tsx`, `frontend/app/globals.css`, `frontend/unit/memory.test.ts`, the Memory e2e.
- **Review fixes:** the Facts bar is drawn to one scale (`depth`, 5 rem for the largest company); a company with facts but no version in the window is a warning, not "Nothing ingested"; the share in memory rounds down; the version bar sizes by flex with a 3 px minimum, and a company with no versions shows an outlined gap bar; the skeleton has the final heading, sort group and column heads; phone width keeps numbers whole and the Company column sticky; open items keep a second condition in their detail and name drift with its count; singular counts.
- **UX laws that decided each block:** Von Restorff (the gap is the one red thing, even when rare), Doherty (the skeleton is the final layout), Tesler (arithmetic lives in the tested lib), Hick (one open list before the table).
- **Recorded choice:** a reconciliation never run is amber, not red (nothing is known to be wrong); the in-memory segment is the accent colour, the one exception to "colour only for gaps and warnings".
- **E2E seed:** the Memory e2e targets the workbench database (Coherent retained into the Hindsight fake; the first database has nothing retained, so its rows tie on facts and the sort can't be seen). Chosen from reading `scripts/e2e.py`, not observed: `scripts/e2e.py` crashed on Windows before seeding (`npx` resolved without a shell, WinError 2), an existing portability gap.
- **Lead's audit:** two layout fixes on production data (company names kept on one line at desktop width; the fact count before its bar). The spec run against production data through a local read-only preview (built export, `GET /api/*` forwarded): 2 passed, the 390 px width included. Not yet run against the seeded databases: the runners do.

## Frontend ticket 09: the reader's way in, nav and research index

- **Files:** `frontend/app/page.tsx` (the index), `frontend/components/question-rows.tsx` (the question row, shared with the dossier), `frontend/lib/research-index.ts` (themes, collapsed questions, status words and tone, the Hypothesis per question), `frontend/lib/routes.ts`, `frontend/app/layout.tsx` (reader nav first, operations apart), `frontend/app/globals.css`, `frontend/unit/research-index.test.ts`, `frontend/e2e/reader.spec.ts`.
- **What the reader sees:** each theme's research questions, newest first, the same question asked again collapsed to one row; a question with a Hypothesis leads with its Thesis Statement (linked to the Hypothesis) and the question under it, one without leads with the question and a Draft pill (a Hypothesis attached to an older run of a collapsed question still leads it). A running run's pill is quiet grey; amber only for what needs a look.
- **UX laws that decided it:** Von Restorff (colour only for gaps and warnings), Hick (one list per theme, operations apart in the nav), Tesler (the collapse, tone and thesis choice live in the tested lib), Doherty (calls go out at once; a failed companies call costs only the names).
- **API gaps:** the research card's headline is not in `InvestigationSummary`, so the index cannot show a run's conclusion without a detail call per row; a Hypothesis is the only conclusion it shows.
- **Review fixes:** the Hypothesis thesis on each row; one shared row component; a pill kept its own width at phone width; no made-up "Company" link for an unknown seed; the `!important` nav rule replaced by a selector; `routes.memory`.
- **Checks:** `npm --prefix frontend run lint`, `typecheck`, `test` (109 passed) and `build` pass. The seeded e2e (`scripts/e2e.py`) was not run here.

The owner's own Chrome (driven through the browser extension) still timed out on `/` and the dossier while `/memory/` and `/companies/` worked; not reproduced elsewhere, left to the owner to check by hand.

## Bottleneck-argument ticket 02: Facts that hold a quantity, a period and a status

- **Files:** `backend/atlas/facts/` (`service.py`: `Facts.create`, `FactValue`, `Quantity`, `get_fact`, `list_facts`), `backend/atlas/api/facts.py` (`POST /api/v1/facts`, `GET /api/v1/facts`, `GET /api/v1/facts/{id}`), `backend/atlas/api/app.py` (router), `backend/atlas/assertions.py` (`Assertions.create` refuses predicate `fact`: `use_facts`), `backend/atlas/investigations/grounding.py` (`number_occurs`), migration `0099_facts` (down_revision `0077`; the lead re-chains), `frontend/lib/api/{openapi.json,schema.ts}`, `tests/unit/test_facts.py`, `tests/integration/test_facts.py`, `tests/integration/test_migrations.py` (head `0099`).
- **What:** a Fact is an Assertion with predicate `fact` and a validated `value_json` (`step`, `statement`, optional `quantity {value, unit, metric}`, optional `period`, `status`), plus an insert-only `fact` row (step, status, optional `investigation_id`) so reads filter by step and investigation. The Assertion's exact span check is unchanged; a quantity's number must occur in the quote (as written, with its magnitude applied, or before it: "$1.01 billion" holds 1.01 and 1010000000), else 422 `quantity_not_in_quote`. One transaction writes the Assertion (`assertion.created`) and the Fact (`fact.created`). The Relationship review's whitelist already refuses `fact` (`predicate_not_whitelisted`, no edge).
- **Tests:** unit suite 1004 passed under WSL (includes the new `tests/unit/test_facts.py`); ruff format and check and strict pyright clean; API client regenerated. The integration tests (record, read and filters, unknown step and status, quantity not in the quote, quote mismatch, unknown investigation, no edge from a Fact, the migration head) were written but not run: no Postgres on this machine.
- **Fixture only:** everything; no live service.
- **Deviation:** a migration was needed (the `investigation_id` filter has no other home on an Assertion). `POST /assertions` with predicate `fact` is refused so an unvalidated Fact cannot be made. Unverified: that the `review_relationships` handler runs without LiteLLM in the no-edge test.
- **Next:** ticket 04's statement-grounding check; an investigation role that proposes Facts.

## CI/CD: staged CI, gated main, Renovate policy, release attestations (7 October)

- **Why:** Renovate's first run (6 October) pushed 13 branches within two minutes. The scale set started up to 10 runners with no resource requests; each run's 8 pytest workers, Postgres and Docker starved the others (pytest at 33% after 40 minutes; 11 minutes alone) and 9 runs passed the 60-minute timeout. The other 4 failed for real reasons (TypeScript 7 unsupported by typescript-eslint; ESLint 10 breaks eslint-plugin-react; postgres:18's new data path; a segfault on Python 3.14) and were closed with the reason. Main had also gone red once on a flaky test.
- **Files:** `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (jobs `static` on GitHub's runners with actionlint, `suite` on the scale set after it, `ci` the aggregate; actions pinned by commit at their current majors; uv, npm and Playwright caches), `.github/workflows/release.yml` (pinned actions; SBOM, BuildKit provenance and a signed build-provenance attestation on the pushed image), `.github/actionlint.yaml`, `scripts/ci.sh` (`static` | `suite` | both), `renovate.json` (3 branches at once, npm minor/patch and weekly lock-file maintenance automerged when green, Python/Postgres/Hindsight never, uv's pins grouped, new actions pinned by digest), `tests/integration/test_memory_retire.py` (the flaky test: the 8-K and its EX-99.1 share an `available_at`, so either leads), `docs/deployment.md` ("CI"), `AGENTS.md`, `README.md`. home-ops: `gha-runner-scale-set-atlas` at most 3 runners requesting 4 CPU / 8 GiB (PR for the owner). GitHub: a ruleset on `main` requiring the `ci` check, no force-push or deletion.
- **Not done:** promoting the CI-built image by digest. The image bakes `ATLAS_VERSION` at build time, so a commit's CI build cannot carry the tag's version; the release still builds once from the tagged commit, now attested.
- **Checks:** actionlint clean on both workflows; `bash -n scripts/ci.sh`; ruff and pyright clean on the test. The integration test and the new workflow ran only in CI (no Docker on this Windows box); results in the push's `ci` run.

## The pilot verdict on 0.4.6 (7 October)

- **What:** the five verdict investigations run on production 0.4.6 under the frozen procedure and reviewed (two blind Opus reviewers per chunk of Claims, the lead adjudicating; the card, the archive-search baseline, the run and a researcher's hour once each). **Verdict: fail** (no investigation meets ticket 02's bar; trust gate fails in four of five; pooled precision 63%, coverage 22%, machine-reviewed edge precision 57%; a researcher's hour found 144 true facts, the cards 16). Report: `docs/pilot-report.md`; reviews: `.scratch/pilot/results.md`; defects: `.scratch/atlas-pilot-fixes/issues/33` to `40`; edges: 87 approved, 29 rejected by ticket 09's rule.
- **Live, on production:** five investigations (about 4.8M MiniMax tokens), read-only recalls (no LLM call), 116 edge reviews. Nothing else written to production.
- **CI:** every push now gets a `ci` result (main's ruleset requires one): a `changes` job skips the stages for a push that only changes the tracker or this log, and the release gate asks for a green `suite` job, not just a green `ci`. actionlint clean; the workflow change runs in this push's own CI.

'limited number of suppliers'): ..."). Rules in `docs/decisions.md`, "A Claim's quote states its predicate as a fact".

Wrong ones rejected: 417642df, e6e8e2b1 (sole_sources, hedged); e41817db, 21b1ab6c, 81b76dc6, 884d539f (manufactures, idiom), 53dbb627 (development of), 8a834754 (conditional); 4ee77ec1 (hedged), 90f3c158, 57c4d1e6, bfd97c03 (qualified_for, qualification under way or asked for); eeddeee4, 4f8df503, 52d2eb2d (revenue or demand ramp), 79c51b8b, cc79b65f (reservation), 51c04dc6 (spare capacity), a5ad2ac1 (a business expanding); 38896e24, 07dcf257 (market in general), acdba88c (despite), 050b6e17, 0e1b82ef (played down). Off-question: 0b7c338f (the same hedged sentence). Right: aa083c3d (the same hedged sentence, judged right as "weak evidence"). The 20 Claims the ticket lists are all among the 24.

The new integration test (`test_a_claim_its_quote_does_not_state_as_fact_is_rejected`) was not run here: no Postgres on this machine; the lead runs it on the runners.
